Because a floor never lowers anything, the better of the two Armor Classes is used, as the player would choose. A creature without a sheet reads a `const` or an `abilityMod` off its block's abilities. It may stand on a combat condition, a level, or an item's effect, and alone: no `flat`, `dice`, `times` or `mode` beside it. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.406.

Swallowing, continued (Capability API 1.407). What an action puts on (`applies`) may carry `"targetHolds": {"conditions"?, "fromSelf"?}`: it goes on only a target already holding one of those conditions, from anybody or from whoever applies it, read as it goes on, so a condition earlier in the same list counts. Unlike an action's own `targetHolds`, the rest of the action still lands on anybody: a kraken's Bite hits whoever it hits, and only one the kraken grapples is swallowed (`{"condition": "swallowed", …, "targetHolds": {"fromSelf": ["grappled"]}}`). `"endsOnTarget": [conditions]` takes those conditions off the target, where whoever applies it had put them on, once it is on: a purple worm's swallow, after which "the grapple ends". `"harmsPuller": {"dice"?, "flat"?, "type"?}` on a condition with `inside` is what a creature trying to pull its holder out takes, win or lose. That pull is a contest with `"pulls": true` and `"targetHolds"` naming the `inside` condition: it is offered on someone inside another creature, reaches through that creature's body, is measured to the creature, and is rolled against the escape DC that hold names (its `escapeDc`), else the contest's own `difficulty`, with no defender. Its `onWin.ends` takes the hold off, letting them out beside the creature. 5e's gelatinous cube is `{"id": "pull_out", "label": "Pull out", "budget": "action", "pulls": true, "targets": "ally", "targetHolds": "engulfed", "attacker": {"checks": ["might"]}, "onWin": {"ends": [{"condition": "engulfed", "on": "target"}]}}`, with the cube's Engulf putting on `engulfed` with `"escapeDc": 12` and `"harmsPuller": {"dice": "3d6", "type": "acid"}`; a water elemental's Whelm names `"escapeDc": 14` and nothing more. Anybody inside another creature is reached at that creature. A combat condition may carry `"takesSourceHarm": {"share"}`: while it holds, its holder takes that share of the damage dealt to whoever put it on, and they keep only the rest, rounded down. A rug of smothering's Damage Transfer is `{"share": 0.5}` on a `smothered` condition its Smother puts on with `"endsWith": "grappled"` and `"heldAtMost": 1` (one target at a time), beside `grappled` with `"escapeDc": 13`; the condition's `harms` gives the 10 (2d6 + 3) bludgeoning at the start of each of the target's turns, and `restrained` and `blinded` go on with `"endsWith": "grappled"` as well. A ruleset using any of these needs Capability API 1.407.

A face rolled again (Capability API 1.408). A combat condition or a level may carry `"rerollsFace": {"face", "rolls"?}`: whenever its holder's own attack roll, save or check (contests, item gates and ability checks the fight rolls) shows `face` on one of the fight's own attack dice, that die is rolled again once and the new face is kept, even if it is the same face again. `rolls` narrows it to some of `"attacks"`, `"saves"` and `"checks"`; it covers all three when left out. With advantage or disadvantage, each die showing the face is rolled again. The log says what was rolled again and what came up. 5e's halfling Lucky is `{"face": 1}` on a level of a derived value the halfling's sheet reads as 1. A fight rolling dice pools has no such die, so it is refused there. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.408.

Harm copied to a condition's source (Capability API 1.409). A combat condition with `"copiesHarmToSource": true` makes whoever put it on take the same damage its holder is dealt, of the same type and after the holder's own resistances, and then reduced by their own. 5e's Warding Bond is a `warded` condition with its +1 to defense and saves, the `resist-all` effect, `"copiesHarmToSource": true`, `"endsWhenSourceDown": true`, and an `applies` entry with `"endsAway": {"beyond": 60}`. Damage passed on this way, or by `takesSourceHarm`, is never passed on again, so two bonds cannot pass the same damage back and forth. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.409.

Pools given back within a budget (Capability API 1.410). A catalog entry's `mechanics` may carry `"recovers": {"group", "upTo", "most"?}`: used, it gives back spent pools of that pool group that its user picks, whose levels add up to no more than `upTo` (a value read off their sheet), none of a level above `most`. A pool's level is its own `level`, else its place in its group. The Game Master names them on the use, `[sheet: op="use" name="Arcane Recovery" recover="Level 1 Slot:2, Level 2 Slot:1"]`; a pool written without a count is one of it, and pools may be named by id or label. The use still pays what the entry costs, its counter row included, and is refused whole when a pool is not of the group or is above `most`, when fewer of it are spent than asked for, or when the levels add up past `upTo`. 5e's Arcane Recovery is `{"group": "slots", "upTo": {"derived": "arcane_recovery_levels"}, "most": 5}`, the derived value being half the wizard level rounded up; Natural Recovery is the same with the druid's. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.410.

A text that follows the sheet, and a pool refilled at initiative (Capability API 1.411). A `scaled` column of a row may be a `text` or `enum` column when it carries `"texts"` in place of `table`: steps of `[threshold, text]`, each starting above the one before, and the cell takes the text of the last step the reference reaches (the first when it reaches none); an enum's texts must be among its values, and `values` still holds the row's starting text. 5e's Font of Inspiration is the Bardic Inspiration counter's `"scaled": {"recharge": {"from": {"field": "bard_level"}, "texts": [[1, "long"], [5, "short"]]}}`. A catalog entry's `mechanics` may carry `"refillsAtInitiative": {"pool", "amount"?}`: as a fight rolls initiative, a sheet holding the entry that has none of that pool left (a live pool by id, or a list row's pool by its name) gets `amount` (1 when left out) back, never past its maximum, and the log says so. 5e's Superior Inspiration is `{"pool": "Bardic Inspiration"}`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.411.

Temporary points as a foe drops (Capability API 1.412). A combat condition or a level may carry `"fellsGrant": {"temporary": <value>}`: when a creature on the other side drops to 0 and its holder was the last to deal it damage, the holder gains that many temporary points, read off their sheet (a creature reads a `const`). Like any temporary points they never stack, so the bigger buffer stands. The fight's health must be a pool of points, not a wound track. 5e's Dark One's Blessing is a level at 1 of a derived fiend warlock level, with `{"temporary": {"derived": "dark_ones_blessing"}}`, a derived value of Charisma modifier + warlock level, at least 1. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.412.

Damage lessened by a value off the sheet, and falls lessened (Capability API 1.413). A modifier to `damage` or `damage-taken` may carry `"add"`, a value read off its holder's sheet (a creature without one reads a `const` or an `abilityMod` off its block), added beside its `dice` and `flat`; with `"minus": true` the dice and the value are both taken away, and a blow never goes below 0. 5e's Deflect Missiles is a reaction on a ranged `hit` putting on a condition that lasts one attack (`endsAfter: "attacked"`) with `{"to": "damage-taken", "blow": "ranged", "dice": "1d10", "add": {"derived": "deflect_missiles"}, "minus": true}`, the derived value being Dexterity modifier + monk level. A `damage-taken` modifier with `"falls": true` lessens the damage of a fall and nothing else, and goes without `attacks`, `blow`, `ability`, `fromSource` and `times`; a condition whose modifier lessened a fall is spent by it. 5e's Slow Fall is a reaction on `falls` putting on a condition with `{"to": "damage-taken", "falls": true, "add": {"derived": "slow_fall"}, "minus": true}`, five times the monk's level. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.413.

Entries changed by a catalog's filter (Capability API 1.414). An amend may name `"catalogs"` in place of `"entry"`, changing every entry of them its holder uses, and `"where": {"filter", "equals"}` narrows that to the entries whose declared filter reads so (a word or number, or one of a list of words, without case). Two more changes go with it. `"damageOnce"`, a value off the holder's sheet, is added to one damage roll of each use: every target of a roll they all take (an area, darts that simply hit), the first hit of an entry that rolls for each target. 5e's Empowered Evocation is `{"catalogs": ["spells"], "where": {"filter": "school", "equals": "evocation"}, "damageOnce": {"abilityMod": "int"}}`. `"halfOnSave": true` makes a save that `negates` an entry's damage leave half of it instead, and nothing else it does: Potent Cantrip, `where` the level is 0. An alteration (`alters`) takes the same `"where"`, fitting only those entries, and two more keys for `spares`: `"sparesByLevel": true` spares as many more as the level the entry is paid at, and `"sparesWhole": true` means those it spares take nothing where a success leaves half (the log says they evade it). 5e's Sculpt Spells is a `utility` with no cost and `{"catalogs": ["spells"], "where": {"filter": "school", "equals": "evocation"}, "spares": {"const": 1}, "sparesByLevel": true, "sparesWhole": true}`; the choice names who in `spares` as for Careful Spell. A ruleset using any of these needs Capability API 1.414.

Damage and healing at their most, and an alteration that harms its user (Capability API 1.415). An alteration's `"maximizes": {"fromLevel"?, "upToLevel"?}` deals the damage of an entry it fits at its most, every die (its clauses' and a higher payment's too) at its top face; it fits only an entry that deals damage, and only one paid at a level within those bounds (a choice outside them is refused as `bad-alter`). Its `"backlash": {"free", "dice", "grows"?, "type"?, "unblockable"?}` harms its user once what it changed is cast, past the first `free` uses of the counter its entry spends with `usesOf` (counted since that counter last came back): `dice` for each level the entry was paid at, and `grows` more a level for each use past the first that harms; `unblockable` passes resistance and immunity. 5e's Overchannel is a `utility` spending an "Overchannel uses" counter that comes back on a long rest, with `{"catalogs": ["spells"], "where": <wizard spells>, "maximizes": {"fromLevel": 1, "upToLevel": 5}, "backlash": {"free": 1, "dice": "2d12", "grows": "1d12", "type": "necrotic", "unblockable": true}}`. An amend's `"maxHeal": true` heals at the most with every entry it amends: Supreme Healing, `{"catalogs": ["spells"], "maxHeal": true}`. A ruleset using any of these needs Capability API 1.415.

A missile caught and thrown back (Capability API 1.416). When a weapon blow is lessened to nothing by a `damage-taken` modifier with `"minus": true` its target holds (Deflect Missiles, 1.413), a `"caught"` moment opens for whoever caught it, once nobody it hurt had anything to answer. A reaction's `"freeSlot"` names an item slot its holder must have one of free (not taken by an item worn or still held) for it to be offered; a creature off a stat block always has. An entry with `"throwsCaught": true` is an attack that rolls to hit with an amount, taken `on` `"caught"`: its damage is the caught missile's dice and type, its own flat part kept. 5e's Deflect Missiles throw is a `free` ability spending 1 ki with `usesOf`, `{"kind": "attack", "attackRoll": true, "amount": {"dice": "1d4"}, "amountAdd": {"abilityMod": "dex"}, "range": 60, "throwsCaught": true, "reaction": {"on": "caught", "at": "chosen", "freeSlot": "hand"}}`. A ruleset using any of these needs Capability API 1.416.

Held off while, saves against what is seen, and no advantage against (Capability API 1.417). A combat condition or a level may carry `"notWhile"`, conditions by id or what a condition does by its effect: it is held off while its holder has any of them, so `"cannot-act"` is incapacitated by anything that does it. `"whenSeen": true` makes its save effects and modifiers to saves count only on a save against something its holder can see (a save with no source counts as seen); it needs one of them beside it. 5e's Danger Sense is a level `{"derived": "barbarian_level", "at": 2, "effects": ["own-saves-advantage"], "saves": ["dex_save"], "whenSeen": true, "notWhile": ["blinded", "deafened", "cannot-act"]}`. The effect `"attacks-against-no-advantage"` keeps advantage off every attack roll against its holder, whatever gave it, while they can act; a disadvantage still counts. 5e's Elusive is a level `{"derived": "rogue_level", "at": 18, "effects": ["attacks-against-no-advantage"]}`. A ruleset using any of these needs Capability API 1.417.

A floor under checks (Capability API 1.418). A combat condition or a level may carry `"checkFloor": {"face"?, "total"?, "proficient"?, "abilities"?, "skills"?}`, in a fight and out of it: a single die's face below `face` counts as `face` (its natural too, so it is no longer a 1), and a total below `total`, a value off the holder's sheet, counts as that value. `proficient: true` narrows it to checks on a skill its holder is trained in, and `abilities` and `skills` to checks made with those. It never touches a save. 5e's Reliable Talent is a level `{"derived": "<rogue level>", "at": 11, "checkFloor": {"face": 10, "proficient": true}}`, and Indomitable Might a level `{"derived": "<barbarian level>", "at": 18, "checkFloor": {"total": {"abilityScore": "str"}, "abilities": ["str"]}}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.418.

What untrained adds, off the sheet (Capability API 1.419). A skill's, save's or section's `untrained` may be `{"add": <value>}`: a check at the first tier adds that value off the sheet, worked out as the sheet is, and shown in the skill's number. An ability's `"checkAdd"` is added to a check made with that ability alone, outside any skill. Like a cap, neither may read a skill or save modifier, nor may any derived value up to the one they read. 5e's Jack of All Trades is a derived `jack` (half the proficiency bonus, rounded down, from 2nd bard level, else 0) as every skill's `untrained` `{"add": {"derived": "jack"}}` and every ability's `checkAdd`, and in the initiative derived value; Remarkable Athlete is the same with half rounded up for Strength, Dexterity and Constitution, taking the larger of the two. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.419. The same minor raises `combat.levels` from 20 entries to 40; a ruleset with more than 20 needs 1.419 too.

A long range on a catalog entry, and a thrown back die (Capability API 1.420). A catalog entry's `mechanics.longRange` is how far it still carries beyond its `range`, in the same unit and further than it; past `range` it is made harder as `combat.ranged` says, as an item's or a creature's long range is, and Distant-style alters stretch both. `throwsCaught` may be `{"die": {"from": <value>, "table": [[level, sides], ...]}}`: the caught missile is thrown back with one die of that size in place of its own dice where that is the better on average, its kind of harm kept. 5e's Deflect Missiles throw back is `"range": 20, "longRange": 60` with `"throwsCaught": {"die": {"from": {"field": "level"}, "table": [[1, 4], [5, 6], [11, 8], [17, 10]]}}`, the missile counting as a monk weapon. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.420.

Healing an amend adds (Capability API 1.421). An amend's `"healAdd": {"add"?, "perLevel"?, "fromLevel"?}` gives every creature an entry it amends heals that much more: `add` off the holder's sheet, plus `perLevel` for each level the entry is paid at, and nothing for an entry paid below `fromLevel`. Its `"healsSelf"` takes the same shape and heals the holder themself, once a use, when the entry heals somebody other than them. 5e's Disciple of Life is `{"catalogs": ["spells"], "healAdd": {"add": {"const": 2}, "perLevel": 1, "fromLevel": 1}}`; Blessed Healer is the same with `healsSelf`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.421.

An attack that is over, and answers that strike or send it on (Capability API 1.422). A reaction's `"on": "attacked"` moment opens once an attack that rolled to hit is over, hit or missed, asked only of the ones it was made at, after any `harmed` or `caught` moment it opened instead; `"missed": true` narrows it to an attack that missed its holder, and `"blow"` to a melee or ranged one. Any moment with somebody behind it may carry `"sourceIs"`, a target test (`types`, `tags`, `upToSize`, `atLeastSize`) whoever caused it must pass. A reaction entry's `"strikesWith": "melee"` is answered with its holder's own best melee weapon strike at whoever caused the moment, made as an opportunity attack is; it deals nothing of its own. Its `"redirects": true`, at an `attacked` moment that `missed` with `"at": "chosen"`, has whoever missed make that same attack again at the creature its holder picks, never themself and within that attack's reach or range. 5e's Retaliation is `{"on": "harmed", "within": 5}` with `strikesWith`; Giant Killer is `{"on": "attacked", "within": 5, "sourceIs": {"atLeastSize": "large"}}` with `strikesWith`; Stand Against the Tide is `{"on": "attacked", "missed": true, "blow": "melee", "at": "chosen"}` with `redirects` and `"targets": "any"`. A ruleset using any of these needs Capability API 1.422.

A sanctuary (Capability API 1.423). A combat condition's `"sanctuary": {"save", "difficulty"?, "from"?, "immuneOnSave"?}` makes whoever aims an attack or a harmful entry at its holder (an area aside, and never its holder themself) first roll `save`. The difficulty is the one the condition was put on with, else `difficulty` read off its holder's sheet. Failed, the attack or entry goes to the nearest other creature it could be aimed at that is not already one of its targets and holds no sanctuary, or is lost if there is none. `from` keeps it to attackers that pass a target test, and `immuneOnSave` leaves one that saved free of it for the rest of the fight. 5e's Sanctuary is a condition with `{"save": "wis_save"}`, put on by the spell with its save difficulty and ended by `endsWhenHolder` attacks and casts; Tranquility is the same condition put on at a long rest, with `"difficulty"` the monk's ki save DC; Nature's Sanctuary is `{"save": "wis_save", "difficulty": <spell save DC>, "from": {"types": ["beast", "plant"]}, "immuneOnSave": true}`, kept on by the feature. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.423.

Ending what a catalog's entries put on (Capability API 1.424). A catalog entry's `"endsSpells": {"catalogs", "count"?, "upTo"?, "above"?}` ends, on each creature it lands on, the conditions entries of those catalogs put on them, the highest level first and at most `count`. One within `upTo` (`"paid"`, the level the entry is paid at, or a number) simply ends; one above it ends only on a check of `above.check` (a value off the user's sheet) against `above.dc` plus its level; with no `upTo`, every one does. One held by its caster's concentration ends the whole of that spell. 5e's Dispel Magic is `{"catalogs": ["spells"], "upTo": "paid", "above": {"check": {"derived": "spellcasting_mod"}, "dc": 10}}`; Cleansing Touch is a `utility` with `{"catalogs": ["spells"], "count": 1}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.424.

Casts that reach a foe, and what a sanctuary stops (Capability API 1.425). An applied condition's `endsWhenHolder` takes `"castsAtFoes": true` beside `casts`: then only a cast from those catalogs that reaches one of its holder's foes ends it, one aimed at a foe or one whose area or harm catches one, so a cleric warded by Sanctuary keeps it while casting Cure Wounds on a friend. A combat condition's `sanctuary` takes `"against": {"attacks"?: true, "casts"?: [catalogs]}` to stop only attacks and harmful entries cast from those catalogs, rather than every harmful entry: 5e's Sanctuary is `{"attacks": true, "casts": ["spells"]}`, Nature's Sanctuary `{"attacks": true}`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.425.

A rest that puts a condition on (Capability API 1.426). A rest's `"puts": [{"condition", "when"?}]` puts each live condition on as it ends, after its `clear`, on a sheet its `when` (`{"value", "atLeast"?, "atMost"?}`) holds for. Name it in `clear.conditions` too and each such rest puts it back fresh; one a fight takes off stays off until then. A combat condition's own `endsWhenHolder` may now also say `"attacks": true` and `"casts": [catalogs]`, with `"castsAtFoes": true` as above, to end it however it was put on, a rest's included, and take it off the sheet. 5e's Tranquility is a long rest's `{"condition": "tranquility", "when": {"value": {"derived": "monk_18"}, "atLeast": 1}}`, its condition a `sanctuary` whose difficulty reads the monk's sheet and whose `endsWhenHolder` is `{"attacks": true, "casts": ["spells"], "castsAtFoes": true}`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.426.

A rest that clears temporary points (Capability API 1.804). A rest's `"clear": {"temp": true}` takes every pool's temporary points away as it ends, after what it restores. Without it a rest leaves them be. 5e's long rest clears them, since temporary hit points last until one. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.804.

Picking what `endsSpells` ends, and ending a lasting area (Capability API 1.427). With `"chosen": true` its user picks which of the target's conditions from those catalogs end, up to `count`, rather than the highest first: 5e's Cleansing Touch is `{"catalogs": ["spells"], "count": 1, "chosen": true}`. With `"zones": true` it may be aimed at a lasting area an entry of those catalogs left standing, within its reach, instead of a creature, and ends it on the same `upTo` and `above` terms, with the concentration that holds it: Dispel Magic on a Wall of Fire or a Cloudkill. `endsSpells` works on an item's `use` and `moreUses` too, as on a catalog entry: a Spell Scroll of Dispel Magic is `{"catalogs": ["spells"], "upTo": 3, "above": {...}, "zones": true}`. A ruleset using `chosen` or `zones` needs Capability API 1.427.

Weapon strikes over an area, and a strike again (Capability API 1.428). A `utility` entry with an `area` and `"strikesWith": "melee"` or `"ranged"` makes its user's best weapon of that kind strike each creature of the other side the area catches (either side with `"targets": "any"`), a separate attack roll and a shot of ammunition for each, stopping when the ammunition runs out; a ranged one with no `range` of its own is set down as far as the weapon carries. 5e's Whirlwind Attack is `{"kind": "utility", "strikesWith": "melee", "area": {"shape": "burst", "size": 5, "around": true}}`, Volley `{"kind": "utility", "strikesWith": "ranged", "area": {"shape": "burst", "size": 10}}`. A combat condition or level's `"strikesAgain": {"within"}` gives its holder, once on each of their turns, after a weapon attack at one creature, a strike with the same weapon for nothing at another creature within `within` of the first and within the weapon's reach: Horde Breaker, `{"within": 5}`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.428.

Choices on the sheet, and values off it on attacks, checks and saves (Capability API 1.429). A text or enum sheet field can hold a choice the Engine reads by its words, comma-separated and without case. A modifier's `"atTypesIn": [fields]` changes its holder's own attacks or damage only against a creature whose type or tag (by id or label) one of those fields names. A combat condition's `"resistIn": [fields]` resists the damage types they name. An amend's `"damageTypeIn": field` changes only an entry dealing damage of the type it names, its own or a clause's. A modifier's `add` (a value off the holder's sheet) may now go on `attacks`, `checks` and `saves` as well as damage, and with `rescue` it is held back like dice. A held-back change to attacks may say `"oncePer": "own-turn"`, counted once on each of its holder's turns and never spent with its condition, and `"orDamage": true`, which puts it on the blow's damage when the attack hits without it; a damage modifier may also say `"oncePer": "own-turn"`. A change to checks may say `"proficient": true` to count only on a skill its holder adds their whole proficiency to, in a fight and out of one. 5e's Foe Slayer is a kept-on condition with `{"to": "attacks", "add": {"abilityMod": "wis"}, "rescue": true, "oncePer": "own-turn", "orDamage": true, "atTypesIn": ["favored_enemies"]}`. Elemental Affinity is an amend `{"catalogs": ["spells"], "damageTypeIn": "draconic_damage_type", "damageOnce": {"abilityMod": "cha"}}` plus a free utility spending 1 sorcery point that puts on an hour's condition with `"resistIn": ["draconic_damage_type"]`. Natural Explorer is an applied (`contextual`) effect `{"modifiers": [{"to": "checks", "abilities": ["int", "wis"], "proficient": true, "add": {"derived": "proficiency_bonus"}}]}`, with the favored terrain written in a text field for the Game Master to read. A ruleset using any of these needs Capability API 1.429.

Defense against an attacker that has hit, and opportunity attacks only (Capability API 1.430). A modifier to `defense` with `"afterHitBy": true` counts only against a creature that has already hit its holder with an attack this turn: 5e's Multiattack Defense is a kept-on condition or level with `{"to": "defense", "flat": 4, "afterHitBy": true}`. A modifier to `attacks-taken` with `"opportunity": true` counts only on an opportunity attack made at its holder as they move away: Escape the Horde is `{"to": "attacks-taken", "mode": "disadvantage", "opportunity": true}`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.430.

Kept up without concentration, and spared only on a made save (Capability API 1.431). An entry's `"sustains": {"budget"}` offers it again on that budget while a condition it put on a creature, lasting some rounds, is still on them: used, it starts those rounds again as if put on now, with no cost, no save and nothing else, and only at a creature it is still on. Its `"oncePerTarget": {"until", "whenSaved": true}` spares only a creature that succeeded on its save until that rest. 5e's Intimidating Presence is a `debuff` on an action at one creature within 30 feet, with a Wisdom save against 8 + proficiency + Charisma, applying frightened `{"duration": {"rounds": 1, "of": "source"}, "endsAway": {"beyond": 60, "unseen": true}}`, `"sustains": {"budget": "action"}` and `"oncePerTarget": {"until": "long", "whenSaved": true}`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.431.

Ways over the ground (Capability API 1.432). A combat condition or a level may say `"ignoresHardGround": true`: on a board, ground hard going of itself (its terrain, and growth) costs its holder a plain step, while the hard going of a lasting area, which a spell makes, still counts in full. Stepping up a level still costs one more. And `"runsAcross": true`: on foot its holder takes steps of any height, paying for the ground stepped onto as a climber does, and crosses water for a plain step, but is never offered water as somewhere to stop unless they swim or fly; a walk cut short on water (held still on the way) sets them down on the nearest open ground, and the log says so. A level read off a derived value holds only while that value is reached, so an armor gate is a derived value. 5e's Land's Stride is a level on a derived value counting a features column (6th-level druids of the Land and 8th-level rangers) with `ignoresHardGround`, and its advantage on saves against Entangle is a save modifier `against` `{"actions": ["Entangle"]}`. Unarmored Movement at 9th level is a level on a derived value multiplying "no armor or shield" by "monk level 9 or more", with `runsAcross`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.432.

Far or out of sight (Capability API 1.433). An applied condition's `"endsAway": {"beyond", "orUnseen": true}` ends it as its holder ends a turn farther than `beyond` from whoever put it on, or unable to see them, whichever it is; it is asked only then, never mid-walk. `"unseen": true` (1.310) still needs both, asked as each step of a walk ends and as each turn ends; one entry says one of the two. 5e's Intimidating Presence applies frightened with `"endsAway": {"beyond": 60, "orUnseen": true}`: "This effect ends if the creature ends its turn out of line of sight or more than 60 feet away from you." A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.433.

A rest's named row, and a gate off the sheet (Capability API 1.434). A rest's `restore` step on `listPools` may name one `row`, matched by the list's name column without regard to case, so only that row's pool comes back; and any restore step may say `"when": {"value", "atLeast", "atMost"}` (one bound or both), a value off the sheet, given back only while it stands within them. 5e's Sorcerous Restoration is a short rest step `{"listPools": "counters", "row": "Sorcery Points", "by": {"const": 4}, "when": {"value": {"field": "level_sorcerer"}, "atLeast": 20}}`: "you regain 4 expended sorcery points whenever you finish a short rest", never past the most. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.434.

Kept in any shape, and components waived (Capability API 1.435). A combat condition or a level may say `"shapeKeeps"`: whatever shape its holder takes keeps these of their own actions as well as what the shape's own `keeps` names, written the same way, by entry (`"<catalogId>/<entryId>"`) or action id; and a shape's `keeps` or `shapeKeeps` may name a whole catalog as `"<catalogId>/*"`. And `"waives"`: the components (of `combat.components`) its holder's uses need none of, so what forbids a component (Silence, a gag) stops nothing they use without it, and a waived component gives nobody away while they hide. 5e's Beast Spells is a level on a derived value reaching 1 at druid 18 with `"shapeKeeps": ["spells/*"]` (Wild Shape's condition can forbid the material component, which a beast cannot provide); Archdruid a level at druid 20 with `"waives": ["verbal", "somatic"]`. A ruleset using either, or a whole catalog in `keeps`, needs Capability API 1.435.

Ended on an idle turn (Capability API 1.436). A combat condition's `"endsIdle": true` takes it off as its holder ends a turn having neither made an attack at a foe nor taken damage since their last turn ended; `{"unless": {"value", "atLeast", "atMost"}}` keeps it on while a value off its holder's sheet stands within its bounds. 5e's Rage is `"endsIdle": {"unless": {"value": {"field": "level_barbarian"}, "atLeast": 15}}`: it "ends early if ... your turn ends and you haven't attacked a hostile creature since your last turn or taken damage since then", until Persistent Rage. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.436.

Kept or waived for some rows only (Capability API 1.437). Beside `shapeKeeps` or `waives`, a condition or level may say `"rowsWhere": {"list", "column", "is": [...]}`: they then reach only actions made from a row of that sheet list whose column holds one of `is`, case aside, and never a racial trait's, an item's or a creature's own. 5e's Beast Spells and Archdruid cover "your druid spells": `"rowsWhere": {"list": "spells", "column": "class", "is": ["Druid"]}`, so a druid 18 / wizard 2 keeps no wizard spell in beast shape. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.437.

No casting or concentrating, and ended by going down (Capability API 1.438). A combat condition's `forbids` may say `"casts": [catalogs]`, so nothing cast from those catalogs (an item's use that casts from one included) is offered while it holds, and `"concentration": true`, so nothing needing concentration is offered and whatever its holder concentrates on ends as it comes on; `components` is no longer required beside them. `endsWhenHolder` (on a condition, or where an action puts one on) may say `"downed": true`: it comes off once its holder is down or out of the fight. 5e's Rage: `"forbids": {"casts": ["spells"], "concentration": true}` and `"endsWhenHolder": {"downed": true}`. From 1.438 too, a row with no cell for the column `rowsWhere` reads (a spell the builder added from a catalog, which carries no class) counts as holding that column's `default`, so 5e's class column, defaulting to `"any"`, matches `"is": ["druid", "any"]`. A ruleset using them needs Capability API 1.438.

Aware of some kinds, from a condition or level (Capability API 1.439). An `aware` (a trait's `hiding.aware`, or now `aware` itself on a combat condition or level, which holds only while that does) may say `"of"`, a target test of `combat.creatureTypes` types, tags or sizes, so only those creatures within `within` cannot hide from its holder, and `"uncovered": true`, so only those with a line to the holder that total cover does not block. Whoever is already hidden is found as the next step ends. 5e's Divine Sense is a condition put on until the end of the paladin's next turn with `"aware": {"within": 60, "of": {"types": ["celestial", "fiend", "undead"]}, "uncovered": true}`, and Primeval Awareness one lasting a minute per slot with `"aware": {"within": 5280, "of": {"types": [...]}}`. A ruleset using them needs Capability API 1.439.

Ended by gaining a condition (Capability API 1.440). `endsWhenHolder` (on a condition, or where an action puts one on) may say `"gains": [conditions]`: it comes off once its holder holds any of them, however that one came on, a condition never naming itself. 5e's Rage ends "if you are knocked unconscious", by a blow or by Sleep while still above 0 hit points: `"endsWhenHolder": {"downed": true, "gains": ["unconscious"]}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.440.

A use that works only on a roll (Capability API 1.441). A catalog entry's mechanics may say `"chance": {"sides", "atMost", "sure", "failUses"}`: once it is paid for, one die of `sides` is rolled, and it works only at or under `atMost` (a number, or a value off its user's sheet). While `sure` (sheet bounds) holds it works without a roll. Failed, it does nothing; with `failUses`, the price paid is given back and a use of that sheet row is spent instead, and the entry is offered only while that row can pay. 5e's Divine Intervention: a counter row of its own that does not recharge on a rest, a "Divine Plea" counter that recharges on a long rest on the same entry, with `"usesOf"` naming its own row so a use spends only that one, and `"chance": {"sides": 100, "atMost": {"field": "level"}, "sure": {"value": {"field": "level"}, "atLeast": 20}, "failUses": "Divine Plea"}`. A creature's action may say `"chance": {"sides": 100, "atMost": 30}`: a demon's 30 percent summoning, its use spent either way. The log says what was rolled. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.441.

Picking which conditions end (Capability API 1.442). A catalog entry's `ends` may say `"chosen": true`: the menu offers, for each target, what it holds of `conditions`, and its user picks which end, up to `count`, as `endsSpells.chosen` does for spells. Nothing picked, the first named it holds end as before. 5e's Greater Restoration ends "one of the following effects": `"ends": {"conditions": ["charmed", "petrified", "cursed", ...], "count": 1, "chosen": true}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.442.

A failed chance spends only the row it names (Capability API 1.443). `failUses` spends that one counter row, as `usesOf` does, and never the other rows of its entry, so Divine Intervention's own row and its Divine Plea row may sit on one entry: a failure leaves the first and spends the second. Rulesets keeping both on one entry should declare Capability API 1.443.

Sensing only presence (Capability API 1.444). A combat condition may say `"presence": {"within": N, "of": {"types": [...]}}`, `within` in the ruleset's distance: as it comes on, and as each of its holder's turns starts, the log says which of those kinds are within that reach, or that none are, and nothing of where they are or how many. It leaves hiding alone. 5e's Primeval Awareness: `"presence": {"within": 5280, "of": {"types": ["aberration", "celestial", "dragon", "elemental", "fey", "fiend", "undead"]}}`, or 31680 in favored terrain. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.444.

Recharging after days of game time (Capability API 1.445). A list's `pools` may say `"rechargeDays": {"<recharge value>": N}`, for values of its `rechargeColumn`: a spent row with that recharge counts the days the sheet's `pass` command says have passed (`[sheet: who="Name" op="pass" days="N"]`, which the Game Master is told of), and fills again once they reach N. A rest leaves the count be. 5e's Divine Intervention: a `week` value on the counters' recharge column, `"rechargeDays": {"week": 7}`, and its own row on `week`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.445.

Tallies (Capability API 1.801). A list's `pools` may name a `tallyColumn`, a yes-or-no or number column: a row with it set (true, or above 0) is a count rather than something to spend. The sheet shows its pool as how many times it has been used since its recharge ("Used 2 times"), with no spend or restore buttons, and so does the Game Master's sheet block; its maximum is only how far it may count. Spending it is what counts a use, so a hang-on's `save.tally` (1.404) names it as before. 5e's Relentless Rage and Overchannel: their counters rows set the column, and a long rest starts the count over. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.801.

Rituals (Capability API 1.807). A list may name a `ritual` column, a yes-or-no column: a row with it ticked may be used outside a fight as a ritual, which the Game Master asks for with `ritual="yes"` on the sheet command `use` (`[sheet: who="Mira" op="use" name="Detect Magic" ritual="yes"]`, told so in its prompt). Used that way it pays none of its pools, neither the cost in its `mechanics` nor a counter row of its own; a priced material it needs is still needed and still used up. A use as a ritual of a row the column does not tick is refused as `not-ritual`. What else a ritual takes, such as its longer casting time, is the story's. 5e's ritual spells: the spells list's `"ritual": "ritual"`, and each ritual spell's row with `"ritual": true`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.807.

Who may cast rituals (Capability API 1.809). A list with a `ritual` column may say `ritualNeeds`, a list of catalog entries (`<catalog>/<entry>`): a row is then used as a ritual only by a sheet that holds a row from one of them, in any list, and is otherwise refused as `not-ritual`. 5e's Ritual Casting: the spells list's `"ritualNeeds"` names each class's Ritual Casting feature, so a sorcerer holding a ritual spell casts it only with a slot. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.809.

How often a rest may be taken (Capability API 1.810). A rest may say `every`, in hours of the game's clock: it is then refused as `rested-already` until that many hours have passed since the start of the last one, as 5e's long rest is once in 24 hours (`"every": 24`). Without `every`, or in a game that keeps no clock, a rest of a kind is refused only when it was taken in the same reply or the one before, or with no time passed since. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.810.

Items drawn as they are used (Capability API 1.808). The items block may say `"drawOnUse": { "categories": [...], "tags": [...] }` (either or both, ids it declares). An item of one of those categories, or carrying one of those tags, that takes slots still takes them only while it is on, and is put on only while they are free: with two hands, two weapons in hand leave no room to equip a third, and a two-handed bow fits beside nothing. But it never has to be put on first to be used. The Use button, the Game Master's `[inventory: action="use"]`, the Engine's own fights' item menu and a ruleset fight's attacks all take it while it is only carried, and using it puts it on as a free swap: where its slots are full, the fewest other items drawn as they are used that hold them are put away first. Nothing else is ever taken off for it, neither armor nor a shield nor a bound cursed item: where only that would make room the use is refused as `slots-full` (the Use button names what is in the way), and a fight does not offer it. A ruleset without it is unchanged: an item that takes slots is used only while worn. 5e's weapons, sheathed or slung until they are swung or shot: `"drawOnUse": { "categories": ["weapon"] }` with a `hands` slot of 2, a one-handed weapon's `"slots": { "hands": 1 }` and a two-handed one's `"slots": { "hands": 2 }`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.808.

In a ruleset fight the screen's In hand row shows what the fighter whose turn it is holds in the slots their weapons take, and lets them take a carried weapon or shield in hand on their own turn by the same rule, whether or not the ruleset says `drawOnUse`: the fewest weapons in its way are put away, nothing else ever is (a shield or armor in the way refuses it, named), and their attacks are read again from what they hold. It spends the turn's one free interaction with an object, the one a dropped thing is picked up with for nothing (1.266), so a second in one turn is refused. Beside each weapon that shoots from the bag, the row picks which stack it shoots first. Both are written back to the inventory as the fight's other item changes are, and neither asks anything more of the ruleset.

What a bare restore may give back. A Game Master's `[sheet: op="restore"]` of a pool that only a rest gives back (a pool one of the ruleset's `rests` gives back, other than health, unless a rest gives it back only by a fixed `by: {"const": N}`, which reads as a point the story hands out too) applies only in a reply where that character takes a rest that gives it back. Otherwise, when their sheet holds exactly one entry whose `recovers` (1.410) names the pool's group, and that entry's `atRest` (1.789), if it has one, is a rest they take in the same reply, the restore is taken as that entry's use with `recover` set to the pool and amount, paying its counter row like any use (refused when it is spent); else it is refused as `rest-only`. Item uses, rests and an entry's own use give pools back as before.

A creature's call by options, for rounds or bound to its caller (Capability API 1.446). A creature action's `calls` may list up to 8 `options` in place of one `creature` and `count`, each `{"creature": "<catalogId>/<entryId>", "count": ..., "label": ...}` or read `from` a catalog, as a catalog entry's calls do; its user picks one as they use it, and the action's `uses` are shared between them. `"rounds": N` sends them away once N rounds have passed, counted from their caller's next turn; `"endsWithCaller": true` as soon as their caller is out; and `"cantCall": true` brings them without any calling actions of their own. 5e's Summon Demon: a vrock's `{"options": [{"creature": "creatures/dretch", "count": {"dice": "2d4"}, "label": "2d4 dretches"}, {"creature": "creatures/vrock", "count": {"flat": 1}, "label": "Another vrock"}], "rounds": 10, "endsWithCaller": true, "cantCall": true}`, with `"uses": {"per": "day", "count": 1}` and its 30 percent `chance`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.446.

Lasting longer for a higher cost (Capability API 1.447). A rounds `duration` on what an entry `applies` may add `"perStep": N`: N rounds more for each step of higher cost it is paid with, as Conjure Animals' `timesPerSteps` counts them (an innate cast counts its level over its base level). 5e's Primeval Awareness, "1 minute per level of the spell slot you expend": `{"rounds": 10, "perStep": 10}` with a 1st-level slot as its cost. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.447.

Putting on one of several (Capability API 1.448). An entry's mechanics may say `"appliesOneOf": true` with two or more `applies`: its user picks one as they use it, like a weapon's mode labelled with the condition's name, and with none picked it puts on the first. It goes without `cureOneOf`, `kindChoice`, `forms`, `calls` and `conjures`, which are picked the same way. 5e's Primeval Awareness: one condition with `"presence": {"within": 5280, ...}` and another, "Primeval Awareness (favored terrain)", with six miles, picked when the ranger is in their favored terrain. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.448.

Sending the called away (Capability API 1.449). A creature action's `calls` may say `"dismissible": true`: its caller then has an action, on the calling action's own budget and offered only while some of those it called are still here, that sends them all away. Those called do not get it, nor any calling action, with `cantCall`. 5e's Summon Demon, "until its summoner dies or dismisses it as an action". A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.449.

By the health left (Capability API 1.450). An entry's `applies` may say `"targetHealthAtMost": N`: put on only a target with N health or less as it lands. Its mechanics may say `"killsAtMost": N` with a `save`: whoever fails it with N or less is killed outright, in place of all else it does to them. And `"dismissesIs"` beside `dismisses` sends away only those that are this. 5e's Divine Word: deafened at 50, blinded at 40, stunned at 30, `"killsAtMost": 20`, and `"dismisses": true, "dismissesIs": {"types": ["celestial", "elemental", "fey", "fiend"]}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.450.

Glyphs (Capability API 1.451). A zone's `detonates` may say `"entered": true`: it goes off as soon as somebody other than its caster walks into any of its cells, with no save of the mover's own, and only so, never as its time runs out; `"by"` (a target test) lets only those set it off. `"within": N` makes it reach everybody within N of its middle when it goes off, in the ruleset's distance, rather than only those in its cells. 5e's Glyph of Warding (explosive runes) is `{"entered": true, "within": 20}` on a small burst with `rounds` of 1000; Symbol, one entry per symbol, `"within": 60`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.451.

Keeping some out (Capability API 1.452). A zone may say `"keepsOut": {"is": <target test>}`: those it matches cannot walk into it (with `"way": "out"`, cannot walk out of it once inside); with `"attacksInto": "disadvantage"` their attacks at whoever stands inside, from outside it, have disadvantage; and `"cantGive"` lists conditions they cannot put on whoever stands inside. 5e's Magic Circle: a 10-foot cylinder against the chosen kinds, `{"is": {"types": ["fiend"]}, "attacksInto": "disadvantage", "cantGive": ["charmed", "frightened"]}`, one entry per kind or `way: "out"` to trap one; Hallow's ward, the same over 60 feet. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.452.

Staying once set off (Capability API 1.453). A zone that `detonates` may say what it `becomes` once it has gone off: `"becomes": {"size": <radius>, "rounds": <n>, "triggers": [...], "lights"?: {"bright": .., "dim": ..}}`. It then stays as a burst of that size around its middle, in the ruleset's own unit, for its own rounds, doing the entry's own effect again at those triggers and shedding that light; it never goes off again, and its rounds running out just end it. 5e's Symbol: set off, the glyph fills a 60-foot sphere with dim light for ten minutes, striking whoever enters it or ends a turn in it, `{"size": 60, "rounds": 100, "triggers": ["enter", "turn-end"], "lights": {"bright": 0, "dim": 60}}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.453.

Spell glyphs (Capability API 1.454). A zone that goes off as somebody `entered` it may say `"stores": true`: casting it, its caster picks one of their own entries paid off the sheet, of the first level or higher and no higher than the glyph is paid at, as a mode, and pays for both. Set off, the stored entry is cast at whoever set it off, or for an entry with an area, at everybody in that area centred on them, in place of the glyph's own effect; it costs nothing then, and what it would hold its caster's concentration for lasts its full time. With no mode picked, the glyph does its own effect. 5e's Glyph of Warding, spell glyph or explosive runes. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.454.

Hidden zones (Capability API 1.455). A zone may say `"hidden": {"check": <check id>}`: its caster's foes are not shown it until one of them finds it. The `search` standard action (so `combat.hiding` and `"search"` in `combat.standard`) then also throws that check, once, and finds every hidden zone of the searcher's foes whose entry's save difficulty the total meets, for the searcher's whole side. 5e's Glyph of Warding and Symbol: `{"check": "investigation"}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.455.

Outlasting its caster (Capability API 1.456). A zone may say `"outlasts": true`: when its caster is defeated or leaves the fight it stays rather than ending with them, counting its rounds down as each round ends, and goes off or strikes as it would have. It is never held by concentration, nor anchored around its caster. 5e's Glyph of Warding and Symbol, which last until set off or dispelled. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.456.

Harming nobody, and fleeing a point (Capability API 1.457). The condition effect `"cannot-harm"` keeps its holder from attacking or using on anybody what harms them: no attack, no harmful entry, nothing aimed at a foe, no grab or shove, no area that deals damage, and no strike as somebody leaves its reach. A condition's behavior may say `"from": "point"`: its `moves` and `keepsOut` are then measured from the point it was put on from (a zone's middle, or where an area was aimed) rather than from whoever put it on. 5e's Symbol: Hopelessness is a condition with `"cannot-harm"`; Fear is frightened with `{"moves": "away", "from": "point"}`, fleeing the glyph. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.457.

Keeping some in (Capability API 1.458). A `keepsOut` with `"way": "out"` wards the other way round: its `attacksInto` and `cantGive` then protect whoever stands outside it from those it holds inside, rather than whoever is inside from those outside. 5e's Magic Circle reversed, "preventing a creature of the specified type from leaving the cylinder and protecting targets outside it": `{"is": {"types": ["fiend"]}, "way": "out", "attacksInto": "disadvantage", "cantGive": ["charmed", "frightened"]}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.458.

Freeing whoever steps in (Capability API 1.459). A `keepsOut` may list conditions it `frees`: whoever steps into it loses those of them that one it keeps out put on them. 5e's Hallow: `{"is": {"types": ["celestial", "elemental", "fey", "fiend", "undead"]}, "cantGive": ["charmed", "frightened", "possessed"], "frees": ["charmed", "frightened", "possessed"]}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.459.

Who a zone works on inside (Capability API 1.460). A zone's `inside` may say whom its conditions and immunities work on, `"is": <target test>`, and a `"save": {"save": <save id>, "ignoresUntilLeaves": true}`: whoever it works on throws that save against the entry's own save difficulty as they enter it and as each of their turns starts there, and once it is made ignores what it does inside until they leave. 5e's Hallow, its extra effect on the chosen kinds with a Charisma save: Courage, Energy Protection or Vulnerability, Fear (`{"conditions": ["frightened"], "is": {...}, "save": {"save": "cha_save", "ignoresUntilLeaves": true}}`). A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.460.

Casting over rounds (Capability API 1.461). An entry's mechanics may say `"castingRounds": <2 to 10>`: used, it is paid for and begun, and its user concentrates on the casting (ending whatever else they concentrated on). As each of their next turns starts they spend the same budget on it again, and as the last is spent it lands as it was aimed. If their concentration is broken, or they cannot act or have no budget left to spend, it fails and what it cost is given back. 5e's spells with a casting time of one minute, such as Symbol: `"castingRounds": 10`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.461.

Set up before a fight (Capability API 1.462). Any entry with a `zone` can already be standing as a fight begins: the Game Master says who set up what before it (`"standing": [{"entry": <name>, "at": "between" | "self", "stores": <name>}]` on that party member or enemy), and the Engine pays for it off their sheet and puts it down as though just cast, between them and their nearest foe or where they stand, held by their concentration where it needs it. A hidden zone is put down unsaid. An entry whose mechanics say `"beforeFight": true` takes longer to cast than a fight lasts, so it is never on the fight menu and stands only this way. 5e's Glyph of Warding (an hour) and Hallow (a day) are `"beforeFight": true`; Symbol, cast in a minute, may be either. A ruleset using `beforeFight` needs Capability API 1.462.

Wasting a turn (Capability API 1.463). A condition's behavior may say `"wastes": {"save": <save id>, "budget": <budget id>}`: as each of its holder's turns starts they throw that save against the difficulty it was put on with, and on a failure that budget is wasted for the turn. 5e's Bestow Curse, the wasted-turn option: `{"save": "wis_save", "budget": "action"}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.463.

Walking and steering (Capability API 1.464). A condition's behavior with `moves` may say `"walks": true`: as each of its holder's turns starts they are walked that way as far as they can, rather than only kept from walking the other way. Its `from` may be `"steered"`: measured from the point whoever put it on last steered it at, and from nothing until they have. An entry whose mechanics say `"steers": <condition id>` (with an `area`, to be aimed) steers every holder of that condition its user put on toward the point it is aimed at, and does nothing else; it is offered only while somebody holds that condition from its user. 5e's Compulsion: the spell puts on a condition with `{"moves": "toward", "from": "steered", "walks": true}`, and a bonus-action entry `"steers"` it. A ruleset using any of these needs Capability API 1.464.

Set up before a fight, with no zone (Capability API 1.465). `"beforeFight": true` may go on any entry, not only one with a zone: it is never on the fight menu. Such an entry, or any other, may be in the Game Master's `standing` list with `"on": [names]`, and what it does is done as the fight starts on those it names that it may be done on (on its user when it names nobody and the entry is for its user or allies), paid for off its user's sheet. 5e's Heroes' Feast, Foresight and Antipathy/Sympathy cast beforehand. An item's use may say `"beforeFight": true` too (Oil of Slipperiness, applied over 10 minutes), and the `standing` list may name it as the menu would (`"Oil of Slipperiness: Apply the Oil"`) or by its use's own label alone (`"Apply the Oil"`); setting it up spends the item as using it would. A ruleset using `beforeFight` on an entry with no zone needs Capability API 1.465.

Kept on the sheet (Capability API 1.798). A `beforeFight` entry that `calls` a creature, used outside a fight with the sheet command `use` (`as="Owl"`, with a creature type after a comma where `asTypes` lets one be picked: `as="Owl, Fey"`), keeps what it called on its user's sheet. The sheet shows it under "With them", the Game Master reads it there, and it joins every fight its user is in without being paid for again, until it is killed or dismissed in a fight, or dismissed outside one (the sheet command `dismiss`, or the sheet's own dismiss button). Using the entry again replaces it. 5e's Find Familiar. It needs no data of its own: an Engine from 1.798 does it for any such entry.

A shape is kept the same way. An entry with `forms` aimed at `self`, used outside a fight with `use` and the form it takes (`as="Wolf"`), keeps its user in that form. It is checked against the forms the entry offers, the same cap and `without` the fight reads. The sheet shows it under "In the shape of" and the Game Master reads it there. The next fight starts with them in it, not paid for again. A shape still held as a fight ends stays, with the form's health and the condition holding it, and that health carries into the next fight; one beaten or ended in the fight is gone. The sheet command `dismiss` with the entry or form name ends it outside a fight. 5e's Wild Shape. It needs no data of its own either.

A chosen kind inside (Capability API 1.466). A zone's `inside` may say `"chooses": "type"` in place of `is`: as it is cast its caster picks one of `combat.creatureTypes` as a mode, and what it does inside works only on creatures of that type; with none picked, on everybody. In the Game Master's `standing` list, `"kind": <type>` picks it. 5e's Hallow, its extra effect on "creatures of a specific sort". A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.466.

Ground that forbids teleporting (Capability API 1.467). A zone's `forbids` may say `"teleport": true` (with or without `components`): nobody it works on inside (its `inside.is` or chosen kind, and not one who made its inside save) teleports into it, out of it or within it, nor leaves the fight by teleporting from inside it. 5e's Hallow, Extradimensional Interference: Misty Step, Dimension Door, Thunder Step. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.467.

A ray for each target (Capability API 1.468). An entry's `"rays": {"dice": "1d8", "rows": [...]}` rolls its dice once for every creature it reaches, and the row the roll lands on (the first whose `atMost` it is at or under) is what strikes them. A row deals the entry's `amount` as its own `damageType`, or no damage without one, and puts on only those of the entry's `applies` it names in its own `applies`; `"again": 2` strikes two more rays, a roll landing on that row again rolled over. Each target throws the entry's `save` once, and every ray striking them reads that one save: half damage on a success, and no conditions. Each ray is logged. It goes with `save` and without `attackRoll`. 5e's Prismatic Spray: `{"atMost": 1, "label": "Red", "damageType": "fire"}` through `{"atMost": 5, "label": "Blue", "damageType": "cold"}`, `{"atMost": 6, "label": "Indigo", "applies": ["restrained"]}`, `{"atMost": 7, "label": "Violet", "applies": ["blinded"]}`, `{"atMost": 8, "label": "Special", "again": 2}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.468.

More conditions to pick from (Capability API 1.469). An entry with `"appliesOneOf": true` may carry up to 12 `applies`, one picked as it is used; without it, still up to 6. 5e's Bestow Curse, its nine curses on one entry. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.469.

Durations and concentration by the cost paid (Capability API 1.470). An `applies` duration's `"bySteps": [100, 4800]` lasts that many rounds instead when the entry is paid one step of higher cost, two, and so on, the last for every step beyond (not with `perStep`). An entry's `"concentrationBelowStep": 2` keeps its `concentration` only when paid fewer than that many steps higher; from there up nobody concentrates on it, and what it puts on lasts its duration. 5e's Bestow Curse: `{"rounds": 10, "bySteps": [100, 4800, 4800, 14400, 14400, 14400]}` with `"concentrationBelowStep": 2`, ten minutes from a 4th-level slot and eight hours, unheld, from a 5th. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.470.

Damage changes with no attack roll (Capability API 1.471). A `damage` or `damage-taken` change counts on attack hits only; with `"alsoUnrolled": true` it counts on damage with no attack roll too, a save's or one that simply lands; a save that halves the entry's own damage leaves what the change adds whole. 5e's Bestow Curse, "your attacks and spells deal an extra 1d8 necrotic damage to the target": `{"to": "damage-taken", "dice": "1d8", "damageType": "necrotic", "fromSource": true, "alsoUnrolled": true}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.471.

Damage changes by the blow's damage type, and spent once (Capability API 1.792). A `damage` or `damage-taken` change with `"onTypes"` (up to 8 damage types, read without case) counts only on a blow that deals one of them, as its own damage or any clause of it; `damageType` still names what the change itself adds. With `"spentOnUse": true` the condition it came from is spent the first time it adds; one an item, a level or the sheet itself gives is never spent. The fight AI counts such a change only when the other side deals one of its types, and a spent one at half. 5e's oil, "if the target takes any fire damage before the oil dries, the target takes an additional 5 fire damage from the burning oil": `{"to": "damage-taken", "flat": 5, "damageType": "fire", "onTypes": ["fire"], "spentOnUse": true, "alsoUnrolled": true}`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.792.

Acting before being walked (Capability API 1.472). A condition's behavior `"walks": "after"` walks its holder `moves` with the movement they have left as they end their turn, in place of as it starts, so they act first; a walk held open by somebody's reach waits for its answers, and the turn is then ended again. 5e's Compulsion, "it can take its action before it moves": `{"moves": "toward", "from": "steered", "walks": "after"}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.472.

Raising the health maximum (Capability API 1.473). An `applies` entry's `"raisesMax": {"dice": "2d10", "flat": 5, "perStep": 5}` (dice, flat or both) raises its holder's health maximum by that while the condition holds, and their health by the same as it goes on: the dice are rolled once, and `perStep` adds that much more for each step of higher cost it was put on with. Put on again, the new raise replaces the old; once the condition ends the maximum falls back, and health above it with it. A sheet keeps the raise in its live state with the condition, so it lasts past the fight as long as the condition does. A wound track has no maximum to raise. 5e's Aid, `{"flat": 5, "perStep": 5}` on its 2nd-level cost; Heroes' Feast, `{"dice": "2d10"}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.473.

Casting other entries (Capability API 1.474). An entry's `"duplicates": {"catalog": "spells", "upTo": 8}` puts every other entry of that catalog whose level is no higher than `upTo` (and not below 1) on its holder's fight menu, each as its own option named after both ("Wish: Fireball"), made with the duplicating entry's numbers and budget, paid for exactly as the duplicating entry is, and needing none of its own components. The duplicating entry itself is on no menu. `"only": [ids]` keeps it to those entries. 5e's Wish, "duplicate any other spell of 8th level or lower". A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.474.

Splitting in two (Capability API 1.475). A creature's `"splits": {"by": ["lightning", "slashing"], "atLeast": 10, "smallest": "medium"}` splits when a blow of one of those damage types leaves it standing with at least `atLeast` health: a second of it appears in the nearest free cell, its turn straight after the first's, and each has half the health the first had, rounded down. In a fight with `combat.sizes` each is one size smaller, and one already smaller than `smallest` does not split. The second starts with none of the first's conditions. 5e's Black Pudding and Ochre Jelly. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.475.

Kept off by what puts it on (Capability API 1.476). A `conditionImmunitiesFrom` entry may name `"catalogs": ["spells"]` in place of `from`, or beside it: its conditions are kept off only when an entry of one of those catalogs would put them on, and one already on stays. 5e's Freedom of Movement, `{"conditions": ["paralyzed", "restrained"], "catalogs": ["spells"]}`: no paralysis or restraint from magic. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.476.

Items that soften a fall or hold conditions off (Capability API 1.477). An item's `worn` or `carried` effect may say `"fallsSafely": true`, as a condition may: its holder takes no falling damage and is not left prone by a fall (5e's Ring of Feather Falling). And `"suppresses": ["sewer_plague"]` holds those conditions off its holder, not ends them: what they do stops, their clocks run on, and they hold again once it is taken off. 5e's Periapt of Health lists its diseases both there and in `conditionImmunities`, so one already caught is held off and no new one is caught. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.477.

Held off on some terrains (Capability API 1.478). A condition's `"whileOffTerrain": ["water"]` holds it off, not ends it, while its holder stands on a tile of one of those terrains or the board's environment is one: what it does stops, its clock runs on, and it holds again once they step off. A fight with no board holds it. 5e's aboleth's curse, which keeps healing off except underwater, is a condition with `"effects": ["cannot-heal"]` and `"whileOffTerrain": ["water"]`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.478.

Cast on an object or an area (Capability API 1.479). A `calls` may say `"initiative": "none"`: what it calls takes no turns and keeps no side standing, so a fight ends as if it were not there. And an entry with `calls` and `applies` may say `"appliesToCalled": true`: what it applies goes on what it calls, with the kind named as it was used (`kindChoice`) remembered, in place of on anybody it is aimed at or catches. 5e's Antipathy/Sympathy cast on an object or an area calls a creature standing for that object (`"initiative": "none"`) and puts its condition on it, so the condition's aura, with `chosenKind`, spreads from the object to the kind named. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.479.

A weapon put in hand let fall (Capability API 1.480). A weapon an entry's `conjures` made, not one it binds, now falls like a held item when a condition's `drops` names a slot it takes or a tag it has (5e's Fear), and is picked up the same way; its maker may still dismiss it. With `"vanishes": {"beyond": 5, "rounds": 10}` on `conjures`, one lying farther than `beyond` (the ruleset's distance) from its maker as each of their turns starts is gone once it has lain that far for `rounds` of them: 5e's pact weapon, "more than 5 feet away from you for 1 minute or more". A use again, a dismissal, or its maker dying ends it as before. A ruleset using `vanishes` needs Capability API 1.480.

Seeing through another's eyes (Capability API 1.481). A combat condition's `"lendsSenses": true` lets whoever put it on see what its holder sees while it holds, as well as what they see themselves, for everything that reads who sees whom: a target that must be seen, an attack at somebody unseen, an aura that needs sight. Somebody hidden from them but not from its holder is seen through it. 5e's familiar seen through is a condition with it put on the familiar as it is called (`appliesToCalled`, 1.479), or by an action of the warlock's (Voice of the Chain Master); Gaze of Two Minds puts it on the willing humanoid touched. What the caster's own senses lose while they look is the entry's `appliesSelf` (blinded and deafened). A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.481.

Longer durations (Capability API 1.482). A duration's `rounds` may now be up to 14400, a day: 5e's Aid for 8 hours is `{"rounds": 4800}`, Heroes' Feast's 24 hours `{"rounds": 14400}`. And any of a duration's `bySteps` may be `"until-dispelled"`: paid that many steps higher or more, what it puts on has no clock and holds until something takes it off. 5e's Bestow Curse is `{"rounds": 10, "bySteps": [100, 4800, 4800, 14400, 14400, "until-dispelled"]}` on its 3rd-level cost. A ruleset with `rounds` past 1000 or an `"until-dispelled"` step needs Capability API 1.482.

Saved against on the caster's turn, and sent away by failing (Capability API 1.483). A `saveEnds` may say `"at": "source-turn-start"`: its save is made as the turn of whoever put it on starts, not on its holder's own. And its `failures` may say `"dismisses": true` in place of `becomes`: after that many failures its holder leaves the fight for good, alive, as a `dismisses` entry sends them (1.236), and whatever they held up ends with them. 5e's Prismatic Spray's Violet ray blinds, `"saveEnds": {"save": "wis_save", "at": "source-turn-start", "failures": {"count": 1, "dismisses": true}}`: a success at the start of the caster's next turn ends the blindness, a failure sends the creature to another plane. A ray row carries it as it carries any `applies`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.483.

Holding off a kind of condition (Capability API 1.484). An item effect's `suppresses` may name a kind of condition (one a condition lists in its `kinds`) as well as a condition: every condition of that kind is held off while it is worn. 5e's Periapt of Health is `{"conditionImmunities": ["disease"], "suppresses": ["disease"]}`, its every disease held off and none caught. A ruleset whose items name a kind there needs Capability API 1.484.

Until dispelled (Capability API 1.485). A condition's `duration` may be `"until-dispelled"`: no clock at all, on until something takes it off (a cure, a dispel, an ending it names), in a fight and after it. It does what `"instant"` does and says it as it reads: 5e's Continual Flame. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.485.

Freedom of movement (Capability API 1.486). A condition's `speedKeptFrom: { "catalogs": [...] }` keeps what entries of those catalogs put on its holder from lowering their speed: a slowing, a halving or a stop ("speed-zero") a spell put on does nothing to their walk, while the same from a net or a grapple still does. Its `slipsFree: { "conditions": [...], "cost": 5, "unlessCatalogs": [...] }` offers its holder, on their turn, to slip free of each condition named, with no check, for `cost` of their walk in the ruleset's own distance (rounded up to whole cells; nothing in a fight that measures no walk); one put on by an entry of `unlessCatalogs` holds. The walk the condition kept from them comes back as it ends, so somebody grappled to a stop slips out and walks on. A creature the Engine plays slips free as its turn begins. An item's `worn`, `carried` or `otherwise` effect may carry all three of `conditionImmunitiesFrom`, `speedKeptFrom` and `slipsFree`: a Ring of Freedom of Movement. 5e's Freedom of Movement is `"conditionImmunitiesFrom": [{ "conditions": ["paralyzed", "restrained"], "catalogs": ["spells"] }]`, `"speedKeptFrom": { "catalogs": ["spells"] }` and `"slipsFree": { "conditions": ["grappled", "restrained"], "cost": 5, "unlessCatalogs": ["spells"] }`. A ruleset using any of them needs Capability API 1.486.

Familiars (Capability API 1.487). An entry's `targetTypes` may say `"calledBySelf": true`: only a creature its user called into the fight, 5e's "your familiar" for Voice of the Chain Master and seeing through its eyes. A call's `asTypes` lists `combat.creatureTypes.types` ids the creature it calls is instead of its own, picked as it is used like a mode, one for each creature it may call: Find Familiar's `["celestial", "fey", "fiend"]`. An entry that only calls now puts its `appliesSelf` on its user too, as every other entry does: Find Familiar's bond. Set up before a fight, an entry done one of several ways names the way in `mode` (by id or name: the owl Find Familiar calls), and an entry that names a creature type as it is used names it in `kind` (Antipathy/Sympathy on an object); one given no way when it needs one is left out and said. A ruleset using `calledBySelf` or `asTypes` needs Capability API 1.487.

Attacks given to a called creature (Capability API 1.488). A condition's `lendsStrikes: { "budget": "reaction" }` lets its holder give up one attack of their attack action for a creature they called to make one of its own attacks, paid out of that budget of its own economy, even while a condition of its keeps it from harming anybody otherwise. It is offered for each attack of each such creature that has somebody to reach, while a strike of the attack action is in hand or the action may still be taken; taking it then spends the action and puts the rest of its strikes in hand. 5e's Pact of the Chain: the familiar's bond `appliesSelf` with `when` reading the pact, and the familiar's own "can't attack" as a `cannot-harm` condition. A creature the Engine plays keeps its own attacks. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.488.

Free action and answers to a hit (Capability API 1.489). An item's `worn`, `carried` or `otherwise` effect may carry `ignoresHardGround: true`, as a condition may: hard going costs its holder no more than plain ground, 5e's Ring of Free Action. A condition's `whenHit: { "blow": "melee", "from": { "types": ["fiend", "undead"] }, "save": { "save": "con_save" }, "applies": [...] }` answers every attack that hits its holder by itself, nobody choosing and nothing spent: only a blow of `blow` (melee or ranged), from a creature that is `from`, and the attacker makes `save` against its `difficulty`, else the save difficulty of the entry that put the condition on, or takes `applies`, held by the same concentration as the condition. 5e's Holy Aura, blinding until the spell ends. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.489.

Layered walls (Capability API 1.490). A zone's `layers` makes a wall or a globe (an entry with an `area` and a `save`) that is crossed one layer at a time: whoever it catches as they move into it (its `triggers`) meets each standing layer in order, each doing the entry's own effect as its row says, as a ray of `rays` does: the entry's amount as the row's `damageType` (none without one), those of the entry's `applies` the row names, and the entry's save, or the row's own `save`, thrown for each layer on its own. `spares` (`caster` or `allies`) crosses it and stands near it unharmed. A layer is destroyed only once every layer before it is gone, as its `endedBy` says: `damage` of at least `atLeast` of its `types` dealt by one blow at one of the wall's cells (an attack may be aimed at them, as at a wall's section) or by one area reaching it; one of its `entries` (`<catalog>/<entry>`) aimed at it, as an `endsSpells` with `zones` is, or with an area reaching it; or `wind`, a zone that `disperses` reaching it. An `endsSpells` aimed at a layered wall does nothing else to it. The zone ends with its last layer. `flash: { "within": 20, "save": "con", "applies": [...], "triggers": ["appears", "enter", "turn-start"] }` puts `applies` on whoever sees the wall within that distance and fails the save at the entry's own difficulty: as it appears, as they move that near, or as their turn starts there. 5e's Prismatic Wall is a wall (or a ring) that `blocks` sight with `triggers: ["enter"]`, seven rows (red fire ended by 25 cold, orange acid ended by wind, yellow lightning by 60 force, green poison by Passwall, blue cold by 25 fire, indigo restrained by Daylight, violet blinded by Dispel Magic) and a Constitution flash that blinds.

Breaking a layer, and answers through an aura (Capability API 1.491). An entry's `endsLayers: true` lets it be aimed at a standing layered wall one of whose layers names it in `endedBy.entries`, within its range, as Dispel Magic is aimed at a lasting area (the option lists the walls under `dispels`, and the choice names one as `dispel`): it destroys that layer once every layer before it is gone, and otherwise leaves the wall be. An entry with nothing else to do in a fight is offered only while such a wall stands within its reach: 5e's Passwall, `{ "kind": "utility", "range": 30, "endsLayers": true }`, on the green layer. A condition's `whenHit` on a creature that holds it through another's `auras` now answers on the terms of the condition that spreads it: the save difficulty of the entry that put that condition on the aura's holder, its source, and its concentration, so a Holy Aura given to each ally in its reach blinds an attacker until the caster's concentration ends.

What a layer stops, and whom it spares as picked (Capability API 1.492). A layered wall's row may name `blocks`, any of `"ranged-nonmagical"`, `"ranged-magical"` and `"spells"`: while that layer stands, a ranged attack (not magical, or magical: a spell, or an entry whose qualities say `magical`) or a spell whose line crosses a cell of the wall is refused as though the line were blocked, and once the layer is gone the next one still standing decides. 5e's Prismatic Wall gives red `["ranged-nonmagical"]`, orange `["ranged-magical"]` and indigo `["spells"]`. For the layers to choose what they stop, give the wall `obscures: {}` rather than `blocks: ["sight"]`, since a wall that blocks sight already refuses every aim through it. `layers.spares` may now be `{ "chosen": N }`: the caster is spared, and as the wall is cast the option lists the creatures that may be picked under `designates` (`count` and `ids`), the choice names up to that many as `designates`, and those creatures pass through and are not caught by the flash for as long as the wall stands. The flash also sees past the wall's own murk, so a wall that obscures still blinds whoever looks at it.

A row that stands for another entry (Capability API 1.493). A catalog entry's row may name `standsFor`, a `<catalog>/<entry>` reference to an entry a catalog feeding the same list holds: the row it writes carries that reference under the reserved `_standsFor` key beside its `_catalog` mark, and a fight reads the row as the entry it stands for, its price, its mechanics and all, while the row still belongs to the entry that wrote it (removing that entry removes the row). 5e's Pact of the Chain writes `{ "list": "spells", "values": { "name": "Find Familiar", "level": 1, "prepared": true }, "standsFor": "spells/find-familiar" }`, and a tiefling's Infernal Legacy writes its Thaumaturgy row the same way. Without it such a row is the feature's own, which has no mechanics, so it is never offered in a fight.

An item's use that breaks a layer (Capability API 1.494). An item's `use` may carry `endsLayers: true` as a spell does, and a utility use that does only that is allowed. It names `endsLayersAs`, the `<catalog>/<entry>` reference a layer's `endedBy.entries` is matched against, since an item is no entry of the catalog the wall names. It is offered only while such a wall stands within its reach and is aimed at it the same way. 5e's scroll of Passwall: `{ "kind": "utility", "budget": "action", "range": 30, "consumes": true, "endsLayers": true, "endsLayersAs": "spells/passwall" }`.

Walking through creatures and solid ground (Capability API 1.495). `combat.sizes.passing` sets the core rule: `apart`, how many steps apart in `order` a foe's size must be from the mover's for its space to be walked through (5e's 2), and `crowded`, what a cell another creature fills costs as ground hard going that many times over (5e's 2, "another creature's space is difficult terrain"). Nobody stops in another's space. A condition or a level may give `passesCreatures`: `"larger"` walks through any foe of a larger size (5e's Halfling Nimbleness), `"any"` through anybody. A condition's `passesObjects` lets its holder walk through solid ground as hard going and stop inside it, and its `harm` (`dice`, `flat`, `damageType`) is dealt with no save when a turn of theirs ends there: a ghost's Incorporeal Movement, `{ "passesCreatures": "any", "passesObjects": { "harm": { "dice": "1d10", "damageType": "force" } } }`, kept on by its block's `keepsOn`.
