An item's `worn` effect may be `{"wornBy": "mount", "keepsOn": ["horseshoes_of_speed"]}`: while its holder wears it and rides a creature, the conditions it keeps on (1.513, a `when` line read off the rider's sheet) go on the mount instead, and nothing of it on the holder, whether they ride or not. Read afresh as they mount or dismount. 5e's Horseshoes of Speed, a condition giving the horse 30 more feet of walking speed, and Horseshoes of a Zephyr, one that `walksOnLiquid`. With `wornBy` the effect carries `keepsOn` and nothing else.

Braced against being moved (Capability API 1.718). A condition may carry `"pushReducedBy": {"by": 10, "budget": "reaction"}`: whenever its holder is moved against their will along the ground (pushed, pulled, or thrust out of an area), they go that much less, in the ruleset's distance, paying 1 of the budget where it names one and only while they have it. Never while up in the air. The log says so. 5e's Dwarven Plate, an item that `keepsOn` such a condition.

Seeing through walls (Capability API 1.719). A sense in `combat.senses` may say `"throughWalls": true`: within its range, a wall between the viewer and what they look at keeps nothing from them. The dark, fogs and invisibility still do, as for eyes. 5e's Ring of X-ray Vision is such a sense, 30 feet while the ring is worn: `{ "id": "xray", "label": "X-ray Vision", "sheet": { "derived": "xray_vision" }, "throughWalls": true }`. Its other limits (lead, thickness, the exhaustion of a second use) are for the Game Master. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.719.

Knocking out (Capability API 1.720). `combat.dying` may carry `"knockOut": {"blow": "melee", "condition": "unconscious"}`: every character gets a free switch, once a turn (and once back, so a misclick is undone), to pull their blows (`label`, "Knock out" by default). While it is on, a melee attack of theirs that drops somebody to 0 knocks them out instead: out of the fight, unconscious and stable, never dying and never slain. An opponent so beaten is out of the fight alive, and the fight's summary marks it `knockedOut`; a character lies stable in the dying `condition` as well. What still slays outright (massive damage, a blow that kills) does. 5e's rule for knocking a creature out.

Items a cast makes (Capability API 1.721). A catalog entry's `mechanics` may carry `"makesItems": {"item": "items/goodberry", "count": 10}`, naming an item of the ruleset: cast by a character in a fight, it puts that many of the item into their hand, used there as any item they hold is (a berry's own `use`), and what is left is written to their bag as the fight ends, beside a stack of it they already carry. 5e's Goodberry, as a `utility` entry that `targets` `"self"`. Outside a fight, the Game Master gives the items with the inventory, and how long they last (a day, for Goodberry) is theirs to keep. An item's own `use` cannot make items.

Rests that do nothing (Capability API 1.722). A condition in `combat.conditions` may say `"blocksRests": ["long"]`, by rest id, or `"all"`: while the sheet holds it, that rest does nothing at all (no pools, tracks, dice, clearing or conditions), and the sheet says "Long rest: no benefit while Cursed". 5e's rakshasa curse, "gains no benefit from finishing a short or long rest", is `"all"` on the condition its claw puts on, lasting until a remove curse ends it. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.722.

Turned back on a die (Capability API 1.723). A condition in `combat.conditions` may carry `"reflects": {"sides": 6, "backFrom": 6, "against": {"catalogs": ["spells"], "entries": ["spells/magic-missile"], "area": ["line"], "attackRoll": "ranged"}}`. A foe's entry of one of those catalogs aimed at its holder that is one of `entries`, has an area of one of `area`'s shapes, or makes a ranged attack roll (`attackRoll`) rolls that die instead: its holder is untouched by it, and on `backFrom` or more it is turned back at its maker, who takes it as if they were its target. Everybody else an area catches takes it as always. The log says which. 5e's tarrasque keeps such a condition on (`keepsOn`) for its Reflective Carapace. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.723.

The target's choice of save (Capability API 1.724). A save, on a creature's action or a catalog entry's `mechanics`, may carry `"or": "dex_save"`: its target makes whichever of `save` and `or` it is better at, and the log names the one made. 5e's bulette's Deadly Leap is "a Strength or Dexterity saving throw (target's choice)": `{"save": "str_save", "or": "dex_save", ...}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.724.

Leaping onto others (Capability API 1.725). A creature's action may carry `"leapsOnto": {"atLeast": 15, "noRoom": "prone"}`. On a board it is picked as a cell, as a jump is: a jump of at least `atLeast` (in the ruleset's distance) on the creature's own movement, as `combat.jumping` and its `jump` allow, landing in a space one or more others fill, foes too. It needs `combat.jumping`, and has no area. Each of those it lands on makes the action's save: on a failure the action's damage and `applies` land as always, and on a success half with `"onSuccess": "half"`. Then each is set down out of its space, beside it. One who saved with no room to be set down takes the `noRoom` condition where it stands. Off a board it is aimed as the action otherwise is. 5e's bulette's Deadly Leap: `{"leapsOnto": {"atLeast": 15, "noRoom": "prone"}, "save": {"save": "str_save", "or": "dex_save", "difficulty": 16, "onSuccess": "half"}, "applies": [{"condition": "prone"}], ...}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.725.

Attacks at targets that are something (Capability API 1.726). A modifier `to` `"attacks"` or `"damage"` may carry `"at"`, a target test as `from` is: the change reaches only the holder's own attacks or damage at a target that is that. 5e's Demon Armor: `{"to": "attacks", "mode": "disadvantage", "at": {"tags": ["demon"]}}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.726.

A creature's action kept up (Capability API 1.727). A creature's action may carry `"sustains": {"budget": "bonus"}`, as a catalog entry's `mechanics` may (1.431): once what it put on a creature is on them, it is kept up on its later turns with that budget, the rounds of what it put on started again, with no save and nothing else spent. It applies a condition lasting some rounds. 5e's harpy's Luring Song. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.727.

A wall a creature's action lays (Capability API 1.728). A creature's action's `area` may be a `"wall"`, as a catalog entry's may (1.177): `size` long, `width` thick, and with `ring` it may be laid as a ring that many across instead, or always with `ringOnly`. A wall only ever stays, so the action has a `zone`, which may `block`, stand in `sections` (1.200) and do what its broken sections do, against the action's own save; it stays where it is laid. 5e's ice devil's Wall of Ice: 30 feet long or a 20-foot dome, sections of AC 5 and 30 health that fire hurts more, and frigid air at a broken one. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.728.

More derived values (Capability API 1.729). A sheet may have up to 800 `derived` values, up from 400, which 5e's armor training outgrew. A ruleset declaring more than 400 needs Capability API 1.729.

An item's use at its own level, and one that stops what it answers (Capability API 1.730). An item's `use` may say the `level` it casts at, a scroll's spell's: whatever reads the level an entry is paid at reads it, a reaction's `"upTo": "paid"` among them. And a `"utility"` use may be nothing but a `reaction` that `cancels`, with `upTo` and `above` as a catalog entry's Counterspell has them (1.116). 5e's scroll of Counterspell: `{"kind": "utility", "budget": "reaction", "consumes": true, "level": 3, "reaction": {"on": "used", "cancels": true, "against": {"catalogs": ["spells"]}, "upTo": "paid", "above": {"check": {"abilityModFromField": "spellcasting_ability"}, "dc": 10}}}` stops a spell of 3rd level or lower outright, and a higher one on its reader's check against 10 plus its level. An item's use with `"upTo": "paid"` has a `level`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.730.

A builder step's filter that lists values (Capability API 1.731). A guided-builder `catalog` step's `filters` may give a filter a list of values, at most 12, and an entry matching any of them is offered: 5e's bard's Magical Secrets, a spell or a cantrip from any class, `{"kind": ["spell", "cantrip"]}`, keeping the catalog's other kinds out. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.731.

What an item does only while something of it is left (Capability API 1.732). An item's `worn` or `carried` effect may say `"whileLeft"`, up to 4 lines of `{"keys": [...], "charges": true}` or `{"keys": [...], "uses": "<a further use's label>"}`: the effect's parts named in `keys` hold only while the item has charges left, or while that further use, one that counts its own uses (1.519), has any. On the sheet they are read as the stack keeps them, and in a fight as the fight spends them. 5e's Helm of Brilliance, `[{"keys": ["resist"], "uses": "Wall of Fire"}, {"keys": ["keepsOn"], "charges": true}]`: fire resistance while a ruby is left, and the light that burns undead while a diamond is. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.732.

A call that ends with its caller, on an entry or an item (Capability API 1.733). A catalog entry's or an item's `calls` may say `"endsWithCaller": true`, as a creature's call may (1.446): those it called leave as soon as their caller is out. `"incapacitated"` also sends them away as soon as their caller is down or holds anything that `cannot-act`. 5e's Feather Token (whip), which "vanishes when you are incapacitated or die": `{"calls": {"creature": "monsters/feather_whip", "count": {"flat": 1}, "rounds": 600, "endsWithCaller": "incapacitated"}}`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.733.

Understanding without being understood (Capability API 1.734). A combat condition's `languages` may be `"understands"`: its holder understands every language, so an action that needs its target to understand its user (`targetTypes.understands`) reaches them, but they are understood by nobody the more for it. `"all"` (1.648) wins over it, and `"none"` over both. 5e's Comprehend Languages, and the Helm of Comprehending Languages' cast of it. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.734.

An aura while ridden (Capability API 1.735). A condition's aura may say `"whileRidden": true`: it spreads only while somebody rides its holder, and its `side` is read from the rider's, so `"enemies"` are the rider's foes. 5e's goat of terror, kept on with `keepsOn`: `{"distance": 30, "side": "enemies", "whileRidden": true, "save": {"save": "wis_save", "difficulty": 15, "applies": [{"condition": "frightened", "duration": {"rounds": 10}}]}}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.735.

Calls commanded by an action (Capability API 1.736). A creature action's `calls` may say `"commandedBy": {"action": "multiattack"}`, naming one of the same creature's actions: a called creature then acts on its turn only when its caller used that action since the called creature's last turn, and otherwise skips it. Creature calls may also say `"endsWithCaller": "incapacitated"`, sending the called creatures away when the caller is incapacitated or dies, as catalog calls could since 1.733. `commandedBy` can't go with `commandedWith`. 5e's chain devil, whose Animate Chains act only through its Multiattack: `{"calls": {"creature": "animated-chain", "count": 4, "initiative": "after", "commandedBy": {"action": "multiattack"}, "endsWithCaller": "incapacitated"}}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.736.

A weapon's bonus moved to defense (Capability API 1.737). An item's `attack` may carry `"bonusToDefense": {"upTo": 3}`: before its wielder's first attack with it on a turn, they may move 1 up to `upTo` of its bonus to their defense, for nothing. Each amount is its own switch (`label` names them, the item's name by default). That turn its attacks lose as much to hit and to damage, and their defense keeps it until the start of their next turn. 5e's Defender, a +3 weapon: `{"attack": {..., "toHit": {..., "bonus": 3}, "damage": {..., "bonus": 3}, "bonusToDefense": {"upTo": 3, "label": "Defender"}}}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.737.

An entry's attack against some targets (Capability API 1.738). A catalog entry's `mechanics`, or an item's `use`, may carry `against` as a weapon's `attack` does (1.187): up to 4 of `{"is", "marked", "toHit", "mode", "ignoresCover", "ignoresLongRange"}`, changing its attack roll at a target that matches. 5e's Shocking Grasp, advantage at a target wearing metal (1.711's `wears`, which reads anything a sheet has on or in hand, a metal weapon included): `{"against": [{"is": {"wears": {"tags": ["metal"]}}, "mode": "advantage"}]}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.738.

Weapons a call lends (Capability API 1.739). An item's `use` (or one of its `moreUses`) that `calls` may carry `"wieldsWhileCalled": {"items": ["gear/goat-horn-lance", "gear/goat-horn-longsword"], "takes": "action"}`: up to 4 weapons of the ruleset (items with an `attack`), which its user wields only while a creature that use called stands. With `takes`, each is first taken in hand on that budget (a "Take" switch), and is theirs while the creature they took it from stands. They count toward no slot and are never written to the bag. 5e's Figurine of Wondrous Power, goat of terror: the goat comes with no attacks of its own, its horns are a +1 lance and a +2 longsword, removing one takes an action, and they are gone when it reverts. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.739.

A condition put on unless its applier already holds one (Capability API 1.740). An entry of `applies` may carry `"unlessImposing": ["grappled"]`, as an action's own (1.715): it goes on only while its applier holds none of those on anybody, and neither does anything of the same list that `endsWith` it. 5e's chain devil's Chain, "grappled (escape DC 14) if the devil isn't already grappling a creature", with its restraint `endsWith` the grapple: `[{"condition": "grappled", "unlessImposing": ["grappled"], ...}, {"condition": "restrained", "endsWith": "grappled", ...}]`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.740.

An area that ends as its maker is incapacitated (Capability API 1.741). A `zone`, on a catalog entry or a creature's action, may say `"endsWithCaller": "incapacitated"`: it ends as soon as its maker is down, out or holds anything that `cannot-act`, besides its rounds running out. Never with `outlasts`. 5e's ice devil's Wall of Ice, "until the devil is incapacitated or dies". A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.741.

A call that ends once it is spent (Capability API 1.742). A catalog entry's or an item use's `calls` may say `"endsWhenSpent": true`: those it called leave once every action of theirs that counts its `uses` has none left, and an item they became (`becomesItem`, 1.613) is back in its holder's hand. 5e's Dancing Sword, "after the hovering sword attacks for the fourth time": the hovering sword's attack with `"uses": {"per": "encounter", "count": 4}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.742.

More senses (Capability API 1.743). `combat.senses` takes up to 12 senses, where it took 8: 5e's Ring of X-ray Vision is its ninth. A ruleset with more than 8 needs Capability API 1.743.

A cost for using an item again before a rest (Capability API 1.744). An item's `use` may say `againBeforeRest` with the `rests` that clear it, a `save` (`save`, `difficulty`), and what failing that save does to the user: `tracks` moved (each `track` and `by`) and `conditions` put on, one or both. The first use since one of those rests costs nothing; each one after it asks the save. The count is kept on the item's stack, in a fight and out of one, and the named rest clears it. The save is rolled only where checks are a dice sum. 5e's Ring of X-ray Vision: `{"rests": ["long"], "save": {"save": "con_save", "difficulty": 15}, "tracks": [{"track": "exhaustion", "by": 1}]}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.744.

Items made by an item's use (Capability API 1.745). An item's `use` may say `makesItems` (`item`, `count`) as a catalog entry's mechanics do (1.721): used in a fight, that many of the item land in its user's hand, used there as any held item is and written to their bag as the fight ends; used outside one, they go straight into the bag beside it. A scroll of Goodberry, `{"item": "gear/goodberry", "count": 10}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.745.

Further uses' counts rolled for each stack (Capability API 1.746). An item may say `moreUsesRolled` with `dice` and `weights`, one weight for each of its `moreUses` in order, each of which counts its own `uses`. Each stack is made with `dice` many pieces, each picked for one further use as likely as its weight against the rest, and each further use has as many uses as pieces were picked for it (never past its `max`; none where none was, and it is then never offered). Thrown once for the stack, from its id, and the same at every reading; its `uses.recharge` brings back that many. 5e's Necklace of Prayer Beads, 1d4 + 2 beads of six kinds: `{"dice": "1d4+2", "weights": [6, 6, 4, 2, 1, 1]}`, each kind a further use with `{"max": 6, "recharge": {"rests": ["long"], "amount": "max"}}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.746.

A further use that spends another's count (Capability API 1.747). One of an item's `moreUses` may say `usesOf` with the label of another further use that counts its own `uses`, and have no `uses` of its own: it is offered while that one has any left, and spends from it, in a fight and out of one. With `moreUsesRolled` its weight is 0. 5e's Necklace of Prayer Beads, a bead of curing: `Cure Wounds` with the bead's `uses`, and `Lesser Restoration` with `"usesOf": "Cure Wounds"`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.747.

A call rolled among what it reads from a catalog (Capability API 1.748). A call (`calls`) with `from` may say `"rolled": true`: as it is used, one of the creatures it reads is chosen for its user, each as likely, rather than picked by them; `keepsRoll` and `lifetimeUses` work as for rolled `options`. Without `rounds` they stay until dismissed or out. 5e's Necklace of Prayer Beads, a bead of summons' Planar Ally, the deity choosing: `{"from": {"catalog": "creatures", "filters": {"type": ["celestial", "elemental", "fiend"]}}, "count": {"flat": 1}, "rolled": true}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.748.

An item of only further uses (Capability API 1.749). An item with `moreUsesRolled` may have no `use` of its own: its menu, in a fight and out of one, is only its further uses, and a further use rolled to none for a stack is never offered. 5e's Necklace of Prayer Beads, whose every bead is one. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.749.

Saves made as days pass that count and harm, and damage kept off healing (Capability API 1.750). A `saveEnds` with `"at": "days"` may say `successes` (2 to 10): the condition ends only after that many made saves, counted across the days on its holder's sheet (a failure resets nothing). It may also say `harmsOnFail: { "dice", "flat", "type" }`, dealt to its holder's health on each failed day's save. A combat condition that says `keepsDamage: true` keeps what it deals itself (its `harms`, and what its day saves harm) off healing while it holds: the damage lowers its holder's health maximum, and no heal or rest gives it back until the condition ends, however it ends. A block's health is kept the same way in a fight. 5e's Pale tincture: `"saveEnds": { "save": "con_save", "at": "days", "days": 1, "successes": 7, "harmsOnFail": { "dice": "1d6", "type": "poison" } }` on its applies, and `keepsDamage` on its condition. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.750.

Conditions until midnight, and an ending's harm saved for half (Capability API 1.751). An applied condition's `lastsDays` may be `"midnight"`: no clock in the fight and, on a sheet still holding it as the fight ends (or put on by an item's use outside one), it ends at the sheet's next `pass` of a day. Its `onEnd.harm` may say `save: { "save", "difficulty", "onSuccess": "half" }`, rolled by its holder as it is dealt, for half on a success, in a fight and as days pass. Outside a fight an ending's harm is dealt when its days run out (`expired`) or its day saves end it (`save`), as `onEnd.when` allows; a cure never deals it, and `when: ["expired"]` keeps a fight's cure from dealing it too. 5e's Midnight tears: `"lastsDays": "midnight"` with `"onEnd": { "harm": { "dice": "9d6", "type": "poison", "save": { "save": "con_save", "difficulty": 17, "onSuccess": "half" } }, "when": ["expired"] }`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.751.

Checks made for a purpose (Capability API 1.752). A `checks` modifier may say `for: ["climb", "swim"]`: it changes only a check the Game Master's tag marks `for="climb"` or `for="swim"` (read without case), never the same skill rolled for anything else, and never a fight's own checks (a grapple or a shove), which are made for no such purpose. The Game Master is told every purpose the ruleset's conditions and catalogs name, and the record keeps `for=`. 5e's Gloves of Swimming and Climbing: `{ "to": "checks", "skills": ["athletics"], "flat": 5, "for": ["climb", "swim"] }` while worn. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.752.

Forbiddance's choices and its bar on teleporting in (Capability API 1.753). A zone's `forbids.teleport` may be `"into"`: nobody at all, whoever the zone works on, teleports into it, while teleporting within it or out of it stays allowed, and nobody carried along is set down in it either. An entry (or an item's use) with a zone may say `"kindChoice": "many"`: its user names one or more of `combat.creatureTypes`' types and tags as it is cast, among those its `targetTypes.is` names where it names any (Forbiddance's five) (its option lists them as `kindsOf`, and the choice names them as `kinds`), and its zone strikes only those, in place of the kinds its `targetTypes` names; named none, it strikes whom its `targetTypes` say. An entry that deals damage may say `"damageTypeChoice"`: two to eight of the ruleset's damage types, offered as modes (`damage-<type>`), the one picked being what it and its zone's strikes deal; it is picked like any mode, so it goes without the other choices made as modes, and cast in a fight with none picked it deals the first. Set up before a fight, the Game Master's `standing` names the kinds as `"kinds": [names]` and the type as `"mode"`; with no type named the first is dealt, and said. 5e's Forbiddance: `"kindChoice": "many"`, `"damageTypeChoice": ["radiant", "necrotic"]`, `"targetTypes": { "is": { "types": ["celestial", "elemental", "fey", "fiend", "undead"] } }` and a zone with `"forbids": { "teleport": "into" }`. A ruleset using any of these needs Capability API 1.753.

Creatures of up to 16 actions (Capability API 1.754). A bestiary creature, and a creature sheet's block, may carry up to 16 actions, 12 before: 5e's vampire, with its bat and mist forms beside its own. A ruleset with a creature of more than 12 needs Capability API 1.754.

Target tests of where one stands (Capability API 1.755). A target test (`targetIs`, a `targetTypes.is` or `not`, and anywhere else one is read) may say `insideZoneOf` or `outsideZoneOf`: entries by id (`magic-circle`, or `spells/magic-circle`) or name, at most 8. `insideZoneOf` is only one standing in a zone one of them left, whoever cast it, and `outsideZoneOf` only one not standing in any. Each may also go in `except` and `anyOf`. Outside a fight, nobody stands in a zone. 5e's Magic Jar, which cannot possess a creature "warded by a protection from evil and good or magic circle spell": `{"outsideZoneOf": ["magic-circle"], "except": {"holding": ["protected_from_evil"]}}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.755.

Wish's stress (Capability API 1.756). A condition's `abilities` may lower one to a number only where it is higher, `{"atMost": 3}`, after everything else changes it (the lowest of several); it never raises one. An applied condition's `lastsDays` may be rolled as it goes on, `{"dice": "2d4"}`, and with `restingCounts` (2 to 7) each day its holder spends resting counts as that many: the Game Master's `[sheet: op="pass" days="N" resting="yes"]` says the days were spent resting. 5e's Wish stress, "your Strength drops to 3, if it isn't 3 or lower already, for 2d4 days. For each of those days that you spend resting and doing nothing more than light activity, your remaining recovery time decreases by 2 days": a condition with `"abilities": {"str": {"atMost": 3}}`, applied with `"duration": "until-dispelled", "lastsDays": {"dice": "2d4", "restingCounts": 2}`. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.756.

Heals of all one is missing (Capability API 1.757). A `heal` with `healsAll: true` gives each target all the health they are missing, whatever `amount` it names (it needs none); it is not shared out (`sharesHeal`) or taken by uses (`usesChosen`). 5e's Wish, "up to twenty creatures that you can see regain all hit points". A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.757.

A ward picked as it is cast (Capability API 1.758). An entry with `applies` may say `wardPick: true`: as it is used, its user picks one entry or creature action that anybody in the fight has (not the contests everybody has, such as a grapple), and each condition it puts on wards its holder off that one alone, from anybody but themselves, at any level. An entry is named `<catalog>/<entry>`, and a creature's action without an entry is named `action:<its name>` in lower case. The menu offers them as a pick of exactly one (the choice's `kinds`, one id), and a creature's own choice takes the first. A condition put on `until-dispelled` keeps what it wards off on its holder's sheet, for later fights. 5e's Wish, "up to ten creatures that you can see immunity to a single spell or other magical effect for 8 hours" (a lich's life drain): `{"kind": "buff", "targets": "ally", "wardPick": true, "applies": [{"condition": "wished_ward", "duration": {"rounds": 4800}}]}`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.758.

Sight through so much of each material (Capability API 1.759). A sense's `throughWalls` may name, in place of `true`, the most inches of each material it sees through (1 to 16 materials, 1 to 1200 inches each): the walls on a line are added up by material, and a material it does not name stops it. A cast wall that blocks sight may say its `material`, `{"of": "stone", "inches": 6}`. A board's own walls (a wall or mountain tile, a prop that blocks sight, risen ground between) are what `combat.sight.boardWalls` says, each cell that thick, such as `{"of": "stone", "inches": 60}`; unsaid, and for a cast wall with no material, such a sense does not see through them. 5e's Ring of X-ray Vision, "1 foot of stone, 1 inch of common metal, or up to 3 feet of wood or dirt. Thicker substances block the vision, as does a thin sheet of lead": `"throughWalls": {"stone": 12, "metal": 1, "wood": 36, "dirt": 36}`, with lead never named; Wall of Stone `{"of": "stone", "inches": 6}`, Wall of Iron `{"of": "metal", "inches": 1}`. A ruleset using any of them needs Capability API 1.759.

What is made lasting a while before it vanishes (Capability API 1.760). A `makesItems` may say how long what it makes `lasts`, in minutes of game time (1 to 5256000, ten years): `"lasts": [{"minutes": 60}]`. What it makes is a stack of its own that never tops up another and is never topped up, and it vanishes from the bag when its time runs out, with an "Items vanished" line. In a fight its time is counted in its maker's turns by `combat.roundSeconds` (6 seconds when unsaid), and what is left of it rides on the stack as the fight ends. With several `lasts`, each with a `label`, its user picks one as they use it: a cast offers each as its own choice, and an item used from the bag has a button for each. 5e's Creation, `"lasts": [{"label": "Vegetable matter", "minutes": 1440}, {"label": "Stone or crystal", "minutes": 720}, {"label": "Precious metals", "minutes": 60}, {"label": "Gems", "minutes": 10}, {"label": "Adamantine or mithral", "minutes": 1}]`. With a ruleset, the Game Master may add such an item too, `[inventory: action="add" item="Rope" lasts="1 hour"]` (minutes, hours, days or weeks). A ruleset using `lasts` needs Capability API 1.760.

How much thought a creature puts into a fight (Capability API 1.761). The Engine plays every creature nobody else does by one of five minds: `mindless` goes at whoever is nearest and stays on them, takes nothing between turns but a strike at one walking away, and never gives ground; `instinct` fights by its nature, the weak first, and minds its own hide once hurt, with no temperament that plans; `cunning` knows the basics; `tactical` plays the fight; `genius` plays it best, never less than a veteran. A creature's mind is its own stat block's `mind`, else its type's (`combat.creatureTypes.types[].mind`), else the tier its score in `combat.minds.ability` reaches (`tiers`, in rising order, each `{"atLeast": n, "mind": ...}`); one that takes legendary actions or has Legendary Resistance is a `genius` where it would be `tactical`. With nothing said, a creature of type `beast` fights by instinct, `construct` or `ooze` is mindless, and anything else plays as it always did. The Game Master may still name a mind for one fight. 5e: `"minds": {"ability": "int", "tiers": [{"atLeast": 0, "mind": "mindless"}, {"atLeast": 3, "mind": "cunning"}, {"atLeast": 8, "mind": "tactical"}, {"atLeast": 15, "mind": "genius"}]}`, beasts `instinct`, constructs and oozes `mindless`, a zombie's or skeleton's own `"mind": "mindless"`; a kraken (Intelligence 22) is then a genius. Whatever its mind, the Engine also weighs the danger a cell puts it in, standing up for a hurt friend an opponent is on, and going at whoever its side is already on; a cautious, cowardly or patient creature, or a hurt animal, weighs dodging, getting away (`disengage`, then running) and hiding against its attacks. A `tactical` or `genius` one keeps what its answers need: it is slow to spend the last slot, use or pool that a reaction it holds (a Counterspell, a Shield) could still be paid from. While no opponent could walk up and strike it before its next turn, it puts up a help that lasts (Shield of Faith, Mage Armor) on itself or a friend ahead of a chip shot. After a shot from afar (a blow with a range and no reach of its own, so a bow or a spell but not a thrown dagger) it steps back, without being struck on the way, to the safest cell from which an opponent is still in range; any creature that thinks at all stays where it shot from rather than walk in to meet anybody it can still shoot. A ruleset using `mind` or `combat.minds` needs Capability API 1.761.

Losing heart (Capability API 1.762). `combat.morale` says when a creature the Engine plays checks its nerve and how. Each of `on` is checked once per creature, as its turn starts after it has happened: `first-fall` once anybody of its side has fallen, `half-down` once half its side has, `leader-down` once a creature of its side whose stat block says `"leader": true` has, `hurt` once its own health is below `hurtBelow` of its most (half when unsaid). The `check` either rolls dice against the creature's stat block `morale` score (`{"roll": "2d6", "score": 7}`, `score` for a creature without one), breaking on a roll above it, or throws a save (`{"save": "wis_save", "difficulty": 10}`), breaking on a failure. Only creatures with a stat block check; a mindless one, a summoned or called one, and one immune to the condition `unlessImmune` names never do. One that breaks flees: it disengages from whoever is beside it (else dashes) and runs for the nearest edge of the board, leaving the fight as its next turn starts there, or at once when there is no board; the fight's summary says it got away. One whose mind is among `surrenders` and has an opponent beside it surrenders instead, out of the fight alive, and the Game Master is told it is the party's captive. Old-School Essentials: `{"check": {"roll": "2d6"}, "on": ["first-fall", "half-down"]}` with each creature's `morale`. 5e (the Dungeon Master's Guide's variant): `{"check": {"save": "wis_save", "difficulty": 10}, "on": ["leader-down", "hurt"], "surrenders": ["cunning", "tactical", "genius"], "unlessImmune": "frightened"}`. Forced behaviour that is a condition, a frenzy or Rötschreck, is the condition's own `behavior` (`nearest`, `moves: "away"` with `walks`). A ruleset using `combat.morale`, or a stat block's `morale` or `leader`, needs Capability API 1.762. Since Capability API 1.775 `on` may also name `surprised`, checked on a turn the creature starts surprised, and `helpless`, checked once a turn of its starts with no way to harm the other side (nothing on its menu to aim at them but a contest such as a grapple or a shove); `"halfDownUnanswered": true` counts `half-down` only while the other side has lost nobody. The Dungeon Master's Guide in full: `{"check": {"save": "wis_save", "difficulty": 10}, "on": ["surprised", "hurt", "helpless", "leader-down", "half-down"], "halfDownUnanswered": true, "surrenders": ["cunning", "tactical", "genius"], "unlessImmune": "frightened"}`. A creature the story names as its side's leader (the encounter's `aiHints.leader`, a bandit chief no bestiary marks) counts for `leader-down` as a stat block's `leader` does. A ruleset naming `surprised` or `helpless`, or using `halfDownUnanswered`, needs Capability API 1.775. `neverSurrender` names creature types of `combat.creatureTypes` that never give themselves up: one of those types that breaks always flees, whatever its mind (5e: `["undead", "fiend"]`). A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.785.

Fight poses (Capability API 1.786). `combat.poses` says what is done by praying and what hides its holder, for the fight screen's marks now and for the art later; the rules never read it. `hidden` lists live conditions that hide whoever holds them: a party member holding one, or hidden by `combat.hiding`, is marked Hidden and told to the screen as sneaking (an opponent's never is). `divine` is what is cast by praying: an ability whose sheet row's `column` holds one of `values` (matched without case), or one of `entries` (`catalog/entry`), whoever uses it; anything named as a Channel Divinity, a turning, Divine Intervention or a prayer is prayed too. A fighter who uses one is marked Praying until their next turn begins, and the log says "Corwin prays: Bless.". 5e: `{ "hidden": ["invisible"], "divine": { "column": "class", "values": ["Cleric", "Paladin"] } }`. Each `hidden` condition must be one of the ruleset's conditions. A ruleset with `combat.poses` needs 1.786.

Four more of 5e's own words (Capability API 1.787). An entry's `"fellsKill": true` (with `damage`) kills outright whoever its damage drops to 0, or finds there, never leaving them dying; one that hangs on at 1 (Death Ward) lives: Disintegrate. A creature action's `"whileFlying": true` is offered only while its creature is up in the air (off the ground on the board, flying where there is none, or able only to fly), and a sequence step naming it is made only then: a wyvern's "While flying, it can use its claws in place of one other attack" is a second Multiattack of bite and claws carrying `whileFlying`. A sequence's `"castsOne": true` lets any one spell of its creature's spellcasting follow this turn without spending the budget again (its uses and slots are still spent; the others are gone once one is cast): a glabrezu's "two attacks with its pincers and casts one spell" is a second Multiattack of the two pincers with it. A rescue's `against` narrows a rescue of `saves` to a save against an entry whose declared filter reads as `where` says (`{"filter": "school", "equals": "necromancy"}`), or against anything a creature of one of `fromTypes` (ids of `combat.creatureTypes`) does, either one enough: a Scarab of Protection, `{"where": {"filter": "school", "equals": "necromancy"}, "fromTypes": ["undead"]}`. A ruleset using any of these needs 1.787.

Builder badges, granted entries, heals outside a fight and item aliases (Capability API 1.788). A `fields` builder step's `unansweredAtDefault` lists fields its badge still waits on while they hold their `default`: an enum whose default means "not chosen yet", 5e's dragon ancestor (`draconic_damage_type`, default `"none"`). A grant's `entries.add` gives entries of another catalog of rows outright, `[{"catalog": "languages", "ids": ["common", "elvish"]}]`, their rows written as a pick's are, and no `entries.choose` offers an entry given that way again: an elf's Common and Elvish, and the Acolyte's two languages leaving Elvish out for an elf. The catalog must be one a builder step reads. A heal's `"outsideFight": true` (with `"targets": "self"` and an `amount`) makes a Game Master's `[sheet: op="use"]` of it heal its user on the sheet too, rolled with its `amountAdd`, on top of what the use spends: Second Wind outside a fight. An item entry's `aliases` are other names it is found by, after every item's own name, wherever a name finds an item (a shop's buy tag, the Game Master's inventory tags): `["Plate"]` for Plate Armor. A ruleset using any of these needs 1.788.

A feature's `recovers` can name a rest in `atRest` (Capability API 1.789): as that rest ends, the in-game sheet offers to use it, with the spent pools of its group listed and the highest that fit under `upTo` picked already, and the rest takes the use with it, paying the feature's own counter. 5e's Arcane Recovery and Natural Recovery, `"recovers": { "group": "slots", "upTo": { "derived": "arcane_recovery_levels" }, "most": 5, "atRest": "short" }`. The rest must be one the ruleset has, and an item's use cannot carry it. A ruleset using it needs 1.789.

An entry can need a priced material in `material` (Capability API 1.790): `{ "label", "worth": { "amount", "unit" }, "items"?, "tags"?, "consumed"? }`. Its user must carry items of one of `items` (`"<catalog>/<entry>"`) or with one of `tags`, worth `worth` between them by their own `cost`; nothing else stands in, a focus or a component pouch included, and a fake never counts. Cast from the sheet, a fight offers it only while they carry enough, and a Game Master's `use` outside one is refused as `no-material: <label>` without it. With `"consumed": true` the cast uses the material up: the one item worth the least while still enough, else the dearest first until enough is taken, out of the bag. 5e's Identify is `"material": { "label": "a pearl worth at least 100 gp", "worth": { "amount": 100, "unit": "gp" }, "tags": ["pearl"] }`; Revivify adds `"consumed": true` to diamonds worth 300 gp. The unit must be one of the ruleset's coins, an item's use cannot carry it, and an item, a creature or another entry that casts the entry for nothing does not need it. A ruleset using it needs 1.790.

How a creature goes about a fight (Capability API 1.763). A `behavior` says, beside its `mind`, how a creature the Engine plays fights: its `temperament` (`reckless`, `cautious`, `opportunistic`, `protective`, `supportive`, `disciplined`, `cowardly`, `patient`, `methodical`, `coordinated`, `fanatical` (1.778), `arrogant` (1.780), `territorial` (1.781), `cruel` (1.782), `honourable` (1.783) or `hungry` (1.784)) and its `role` (`bruiser`, `bulwark`, `skirmisher`, `marksman`, `spellcaster`, `supporter` or `controller`) in place of the ones it would be dealt; whom it goes for first, `targets`, up to four in order of `nearest`, `weakest`, `wounded` (below half its health), `strongest` (the most dangerous), `casters` (anybody with a spell or a held one), `healers`, `attacker` (whoever last hurt it), `concentrating` and `leader` (a stat block's `"leader": true`); `opens`, up to four of its own action ids or catalog entries (`fireball` or `spells/fireball`), the first of them it can use and that is worth anything taken on its first turns, once; `pack: true`, going for whoever a friend of its stands beside; and `ambush: true`, hiding where it can and keeping still until an opponent stands where it could strike them this turn, walking first, or it is hurt, after which it never waits again. A `behavior` may sit on `combat` (every creature), on a creature type in `combat.creatureTypes.types`, and on a creature's own stat block, each key over the one before it. A Game Master's temperament for one fight wins over its own, and a mindless creature has none of it. 5e: a wolf `{"targets": ["wounded"], "pack": true}`, a goblin `{"temperament": "cowardly", "targets": ["weakest"]}`, a young dragon `{"opens": ["fire_breath"]}`, a ghoul `{"ambush": true}`, a mage `{"role": "controller", "targets": ["casters"]}`. A ruleset using such a `behavior` needs Capability API 1.763.

What each role does (combat AI A1). A `bruiser` just fights. A `bulwark` stands up for friends: it weighs most what keeps an opponent off one, and ends its walk beside a friend an opponent is on. A `skirmisher` weighs finishing blows and joining its side's attack more, and likes to strike from across a target from a friend already on it (a flank). Having struck up close, a `skirmisher` (or a `cautious` or `cowardly` creature) gets away again with walk it has left: by a Disengage it can still take (a goblin's Nimble Escape), else only to a cell it reaches without drawing a blow. A `marksman` minds the danger it stands in more. A `spellcaster` keeps its best for when it matters and weighs setting up more. A `controller` weighs holding people down most. A `supporter` weighs help more and keeps close to the hurt. A `solo` (Capability API 1.776) fights a whole side alone: it spends what it has and does not mind a blow. A creature whose data names no `role` is dealt one off what it can do: `solo` for one with moves of its own between turns (`signaturePoints`) or legendary resistance, `supporter` for one that heals others, `controller` or `spellcaster` for a caster, `marksman` when its best blow is a shot, `bulwark` for one with a reaction for a friend (a Shield Guardian's Shield) or one that turns a blow aside (a reaction to being hit that harms nobody and puts on it nothing but conditions raising its `defense`, such as 5e's Parry), `skirmisher` for one with a free Disengage or Hide (Nimble Escape), a kept condition with `flyby`, or feet much quicker than its side's on blows no harder than theirs, and `bruiser` otherwise. Its temperament is dealt with that role in mind. A ruleset naming the role `solo` needs Capability API 1.776.

Bodyguards (Capability API 1.777). A `behavior` may say `guards`, one to three of `leader` (a stat block's `"leader": true`, or one the story marks its side's leader), `casters` (anybody with a spell) and `healers` (anybody who heals others): the creature keeps within three squares of the nearest friend of those kinds still standing and goes first at whoever is on them, as a companion told to protect them does; with nobody of those kinds left it fights as it would. It is a standing order, so a mindless creature (a Shield Guardian, a zombie) keeps it too, striking whoever is on its charge when it can. 5e: a noble's guard `{"guards": ["leader"]}`, a cult fanatic's followers `{"guards": ["casters"]}`. A ruleset using `guards` needs Capability API 1.777.

Leaders (combat AI A6, no Capability minor). A creature that leads its side (a stat block's `"leader": true`, or one the story marks) opens, on its first two rounds, with what helps several of its side at once (an option on friends with more than one target or an area: a battle cry, Bless; or one putting a condition on itself whose `auras` reach its own side: 5e's Leadership), its side goes first at whoever it last went at, and, in a ruleset with `combat.morale`, each of its side running from the fight within six squares of it stops as its turn starts and checks its nerve again on its own next turn.

Fanatics (Capability API 1.778). A `behavior.temperament` of `fanatical` is never dealt by chance, only named by a creature's data or by the story: it hits as hard as it can whatever danger it stands in, stands up for its side, never checks `combat.morale`, and in Classic and Tactical fights has no morale score. A `disciplined` creature (no minor) keeps formation, weighing going where its side goes, carries out its leader's choice of target, and is not shaken by the first of its side to fall (`first-fall`). 5e: a cult fanatic and its cultists `{"temperament": "fanatical"}`. A ruleset naming `fanatical` needs Capability API 1.778.

A temperament that turns (Capability API 1.779). A `behavior` may say `shifts`, one to four of `{"on": trigger, "to": temperament}` with each trigger once: `bloodied` (below half its health), `ally-down` (one of its side down or gone), `leader-down` (its side's leader down) or `cornered` (below a quarter, an opponent beside it and nowhere to run). As each of its turns starts, the first whose trigger has happened turns its temperament, once a fight, after which its own `temperament` no longer applies. A Game Master's `shifts` for one fight win over it. A mindless creature never turns. 5e: an arrogant dragon `{"temperament": "opportunistic", "shifts": [{"on": "bloodied", "to": "methodical"}]}`, a desperate bandit `{"shifts": [{"on": "cornered", "to": "reckless"}]}`. A ruleset using `shifts` needs Capability API 1.779.

Arrogant creatures (Capability API 1.780). A `behavior.temperament` of `arrogant` is never dealt by chance, only named by a creature's data or by the story: it takes little care, sets nothing up and keeps its costly options (spell slots, limited uses) back, even when it fights a side alone. Unless its `shifts` say otherwise, it turns `methodical` once `bloodied` or once one of its side falls (`ally-down`). An arrogant (or once arrogant) creature that flies and is legendary (a signature move or Legendary Resistance) takes to the sky below a quarter of its health and fights from there, never coming down to walk at anybody. 5e: an adult red dragon, a lich or a vampire `{"temperament": "arrogant"}`. A ruleset naming `arrogant`, as a temperament or as a shift's `to`, needs Capability API 1.780.

Territorial creatures (Capability API 1.781). A `behavior.temperament` of `territorial` is never dealt by chance, only named by a creature's data or by the story: it keeps within `behavior.territory` squares (1 to 40, 6 unless it says) of where it stood when the fight began, striking and shooting whoever comes onto that ground and walking back onto it when pushed off. It leaves it only to go after whoever last hurt it while they stand off its ground, struck from afar. A mindless creature whose data names it `territorial` keeps its ground too, as a golem set to guard a place does. 5e: a golem guarding a gate `{"temperament": "territorial", "territory": 3}`, a treant in its grove `{"temperament": "territorial"}`. A ruleset naming `territorial` (as a temperament or a shift's `to`) or a `territory` needs Capability API 1.781.

Cruel creatures (Capability API 1.782). A `behavior.temperament` of `cruel` is never dealt by chance, only named by a creature's data or by the story: it finishes whoever it can and likes to hold them down or frighten them first. Where the player's game turns on "Let cruel opponents strike the fallen" (off by default), it may also attack a character who is already down and not yet dead; where it is off, it plays as an `opportunistic` one and leaves the fallen be, as every other opponent the Engine plays does. 5e: a ghoul, a hag or a devil `{"temperament": "cruel"}`. A ruleset naming `cruel`, as a temperament or a shift's `to`, needs Capability API 1.782.

Honourable creatures (Capability API 1.783). A `behavior.temperament` of `honourable` is never dealt by chance, only named by a creature's data or by the story: it fights whoever is fighting it (beside it, or whoever last hurt it) before anybody else, takes no joy in the kill, and never strikes anybody running from the fight (`routed`), down, or who gave up. While every opponent still standing is honourable, the party's menu offers **Surrender**: the fight ends as a defeat in which the party is taken alive, and the Game Master is told so. 5e: a knight, a metallic dragon or a celestial `{"temperament": "honourable"}`. A ruleset naming `honourable`, as a temperament or a shift's `to`, needs Capability API 1.783.

Hungry creatures (Capability API 1.784). A `behavior.temperament` of `hungry` is never dealt by chance, only named by a creature's data or by the story, and an animal may have it: it picks one victim and keeps at them, taking hold where it can, and once it holds them by a condition that drags (`dragged`, 1.497) it hauls them off, away from everybody else, before it strikes again, and walks at nobody else while it has them. 5e: a giant crocodile, a roper or a grick `{"temperament": "hungry"}`, with its grapple's `grappled` dragging. A ruleset naming `hungry`, as a temperament or a shift's `to`, needs Capability API 1.784.

Fliers keep out of reach (combat AI A7, no Capability minor). On a board with levels (`combat.elevation.levelHeight`), a creature the Engine plays that can fly and has something to shoot with takes to the air when it would otherwise end its turn on the ground with an opponent about who cannot fly. Not a `bruiser`, a `reckless` creature or a mindless one. A condition's aura on its holder's own side is also weighed as what it gives each friend in reach, so a self-cast Leadership is worth casting.

Zones caught only by moving into them (Capability API 1.764). By default a zone's `enter` catches whoever it newly covers: a creature walking in, and one the zone comes onto, carried with its caster, moved by them or turned to face them; the log says the zone reached them. `"enterOnlyByMoving": true` on a `zone` leaves out the second: only a creature that moves into it, by its own walk or by being moved, is caught by `enter`. 5e's Spirit Guardians, `"zone": { "rounds": 100, "anchor": "caster", "triggers": ["enter", "turn-start"], "atCast": false, "enterOnlyByMoving": true }`, and Moonbeam. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.764.

A condition that puts others on with it (Capability API 1.765). A `combat.conditions` entry may say `alsoApplies`, up to four other conditions of the ruleset's: whenever it goes on, each of them goes on too, unless its holder already has it or is immune, lasting as a condition does when nothing says how long, until what ends it on its own. They stay when it comes off. 5e's unconscious, `"alsoApplies": ["prone"]`: a creature that drops is prone, and still prone once healed until it spends movement to stand up. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.765.

A builder step that takes all it offers (Capability API 1.766). A `catalog` step with `"takesAll": true` takes every entry it offers without asking: the class features a character's levels give. Of each `exclusive` group it offers, the player picks one, the latest pick counting, and a group not yet picked is what the step waits on; `min`, `max` and `countFrom` say nothing for it, and it has no `pool`, `cost`, `quotas`, `distinctBy` or `quotaBelow` (a `field` needs 1.773, below). A step's `upTo` may also be read for each entry, with `per`: `{ "filter": "class_id", "prefix": "level_" }` limits an entry to the sheet's field or derived value named `level_` and the entry's own `class_id`, the highest of those the sheet has, and to `upTo.value` for an entry naming none. 5e's class features, `{ "kind": "catalog", "catalog": "class_features", "takesAll": true, "upTo": { "filter": "level", "value": { "field": "level" }, "per": { "filter": "class_id", "prefix": "level_" } } }`: a fighter 4 / wizard 1 takes Second Wind, Action Surge and Arcane Recovery, and picks an Ability Score Improvement or a feat. A ruleset using either needs Capability API 1.766.

A builder step that marks rows (Capability API 1.767). A step of `"kind": "mark"` offers the rows already on one of the sheet's `list`s that a catalog wrote there (a row the player typed in is not offered), those matching its `where` (1 to 4 columns, each a value or `{ "atLeast": n }` for a number column), and each row the player marks has its boolean `column` ticked. It says how many with `max` or `countFrom`, exactly one, and never waits on more than there are rows. Unmarking clears only a tick the builder made; one the player changed on the sheet since stays theirs. 5e's wizard, after the spellbook step, `{ "id": "prepared", "kind": "mark", "list": "spells", "column": "prepared", "where": { "level": { "atLeast": 1 } }, "countFrom": { "derived": "spells_prepared_wizard" } }`. A ruleset using it needs Capability API 1.767.

Bigger builders (Capability API 1.768). A guided builder may have up to 32 steps, up from 24, and a `catalog` step's `min` and `max` may go up to 60, up from 40: 5e's wizard has a Spellbook step of its own, up to the 44 spells a 20th-level wizard has written, and the `mark` step after it. A ruleset with more than 24 steps, or a catalog step asking for or taking more than 40 entries, needs Capability API 1.768.

Catalog variants (Capability API 1.769). A catalog entry may list `variants`, 1 to 32 ids of other entries of the same catalog, each of which says `variantOf` back with this entry's id: Chromatic Orb's acid, cold, fire, lightning, poison and thunder, each with its own `mechanics`. A builder step and the catalog picker offer only the parent; a variant is never picked on its own. A sheet row of the parent is each of its variants in a fight, every one on the menu (and offered to a Game Master's command) by the variant's own `label`, cast from the same row with the same slots and numbers; the parent itself is on the menu too when it has `mechanics` of its own. A fight keeps the variants of every entry a sheet's rows came from. A creature's `spellcasting` spell naming the parent is each of its variants the same way, every one cast from that spell's own `uses` and slots, and the parent needs no `mechanics` of its own there when a variant has them. A variant has no variants of its own, and an import refuses a variant its parent does not list or a parent naming an entry that does not name it back. A ruleset using either key needs Capability API 1.769.

Cover from what stands in the way only (Capability API 1.770). By default a target standing on ground that gives cover (a forest, ruins) has half cover against an attack from that ground alone. `combat.cover.groundUnderTarget: false` drops that, so cover is only what stands between the attacker and the target: creatures where `creatures` says so, and whatever else lies between them. 5e's cover is what is in the way, not underfoot. Leaving it out, or `true`, keeps the ground rule. A ruleset using the key needs Capability API 1.770.

Help against an opponent beside the helper (Capability API 1.771). By default Help gives the next attack of the helped ally an edge against anybody, wherever the helper stands. `combat.standardEffects.help: { "beside": true }` makes it 5e's Help: it is offered only while the helper has an opponent within 5 feet, it counts only for an attack against an opponent who was beside the helper when they helped, and it is spent only by such an attack. Name it only when your `standard` list has `help`. A ruleset using the key needs Capability API 1.771.

A group's default (Capability API 1.772). A step that takes all it offers (1.766) waits on each `exclusive` group it offers until the player picks one of it. A catalog entry with `"exclusiveDefault": true` is taken of its group while the player has picked none of it, so the step waits on nothing, and picking another entry of the group still replaces it: 5e's Ability Score Improvement, which a feat may replace under the optional feats rule. As with a pick (1.100), the default is no part of the sheet another entry of its group is judged on, so the improvement a feat replaces never meets that feat's prerequisite. A group of which only one entry is offered (the other's `offeredWhen` reads 0) takes that entry the same way, with or without the key. Only an entry naming an `exclusive` group may say it, and a catalog has one default for each group. A ruleset using the key needs Capability API 1.772.

What a step that takes all it offers took, written to a field (Capability API 1.773). A `catalog` step with `"takesAll": true` may name a `field`, as any catalog step may: a text field gets the labels of every entry it takes, the picked one of each `exclusive` group among them, joined with ", "; an enum field the first taken entry that is one of its values. It is written again whenever what the step takes changes, under the same rule as any step's field (only while it is blank or still what the builder wrote). 5e's subclasses, whose features come with the class: `{ "id": "subclass", "kind": "catalog", "catalog": "features", "takesAll": true, "filters": { "kind": "subclass" }, "within": { "step": "class", "filter": "class_id" }, "upTo": { ... }, "field": "subclass" }` writes "Champion, School of Evocation" for a fighter 3 / wizard 2. A ruleset with a `field` on such a step needs Capability API 1.773.

How the Engine weighs what a spell or an ability puts on somebody. It reads the ruleset's own `combat.conditions`: each effect is worth a share of a turn (`cannot-act` a whole one, disadvantage on attacks about a third), a `behavior` that makes its holder run, wander or waste a turn is worth more, a condition that ends on damage less, all scaled by how long it lasts, the chance the target fails the save on a d20, and how dangerous the target is. So a hold goes on the opponent that hits hardest, never on one already holding the condition or immune to it, and a blast that would also catch its maker's own side is weighed against them. Starting another held spell counts the one it would let go of, and hurting someone who holds one up is worth a little more. An answer that cancels (Counterspell) is worth what it stops: the harm and conditions it would put on its maker's side, or the healing it would give the other. Nothing here needs a newer Capability API.

A sequence's reach (from Capability API 1.740's Engine on, no minor of its own). A sequence with no `reach` or `range` of its own, a creature's Multiattack, reaches as far as its farthest part, and each part is made only at a target it reaches itself: an adult dragon's Multiattack offered at a foe 10 feet off, its bite made there and its claws only at whom they reach.

A copy of a party member (from Capability API 1.726's Engine on). The Game Master's `[party_change: character="Lia's Simulacrum" change="add" copy_of="Lia" condition="simulacrum"]` adds a game character of the new name to the party with the member's card and sheet build, fresh, holding the ruleset's `condition` from the start; nothing is generated for it. 5e's Simulacrum: an entry whose Game Master text asks for that tag, and a lasting condition whose modifier halves health's maximum, `{"to": "health-max", "times": 0.5}`. It needs no Capability API of its own: the tag is the Game Master's. From Capability API 1.744's Engine on, `copy_of` may instead name a creature of the ruleset's bestiaries (by label, id or `<catalog>/<entry>`): a beast or a stranger copied joins the party as a card naming that creature, with no sheet and no items, and fights as that creature on the party's side. Its conditions and its health are kept in its live state between fights, as a sheet's are, so the halving condition holds and a copy brought low stays low.

A condition, or an item worn or carried, with `"alerts": 15` gives away whoever moves hidden within that distance of its holder: they are hidden from nobody, logged as given away by its holder. The showcase's Wind-Chime is an item carried with `{"alerts": 15}`. A creature's `"stillIn": ["bright"]` keeps it from moving while any of its cells is in one of those lights: no walking is on its menu there. The showcase's Lantern-Shy.

Growing auras, holds with a most, tearing free and hanging on (Capability API 1.320). An aura's `"grows": {"by": 10, "upTo": 40}` reaches `by` further as each of its holder's turns starts, to no more than `upTo`, which is more than its `distance`; the log says how far it reaches now. Its `harms` may say `"on": "their-turn"`: dealt as each turn of whoever it reaches starts, in place of all at once as its holder's turn starts. `"lingers": true` leaves what it spreads on whoever it no longer reaches until their next turn starts, and the aura holds it again if it reaches them before then. The Owed's Frost Debt is `{"distance": 20, "side": "enemies", "conditions": ["no_heal"], "grows": {"by": 10, "upTo": 40}, "lingers": true, "harms": {"dice": "2d6", "type": "cold", "on": "their-turn"}}`.

What an action puts on may carry `"heldAtMost": 2`: while its source holds that many others with it, it does not go on a third (logged as not going on), as The Owed's Grasps. A condition's `shakeOff` with `"self": true` lets its holder end it too, spending the budget with no check: the Seed Hopper, `{"budget": "action", "self": true}`, torn free by the held or an ally beside them. A creature's `"hangsOn": {"uses": 1, "extraTurn": true, "label": "Hourglass"}` is left at 1 hit point instead of dropping to 0, `uses` times a fight, but not against what slays outright; with `extraTurn` it takes a turn of its own straight after the one it happened in, and the order is as it was once that turn is over.

The picker shows this block as one line. Who reads the rest depends on which block your ruleset opted in with:

- With a [`combat` block](ruleset-combat.md#combat-a-fight-your-own-rules-resolve), the fight reads its combat effects. `range`, `area` and `friendlyFire` apply on a battlefield with positions; `reaction` marks an entry as one that answers something rather than one taken on a turn, and names the moment it waits for (see [Windows](ruleset-combat.md#windows-holding-the-fight-open)); one marked plain `true` names none and is on no menu. `check` applies to skill checks, as described above.
- With only a [`battle` block](ruleset-combat.md#battles-lending-the-sheet-to-marinaras-combat), a battle reads `kind`, `range`, `area`, `friendlyFire`, `amount`, `damageType` and `cost`, because those are the parts Marinara's own combat has somewhere to put.

The vocabulary is closed, so a key or a value that is not in the list above is refused instead of being quietly ignored.

`cost` is also what the Game Master's `use` command pays, outside battle: see [The `use` command](ruleset-catalogs.md#the-use-command-letting-the-game-master-spend-a-price-you-wrote).
